<section id="overview"><div class="eyebrow">01 / Starting position</div><h1>A working agency,<br>seen from five sides.</h1><p class="lead">Make the work satisfying. Let the discrepancies become decisions. Keep the record precise enough to disagree with.</p><div class="ledegrid"><div class="intro-detail"><h3>The park is the operating world.</h3><p>Projects occupy it. Departments keep it moving. Clients commission work. Suppliers provide it. Companies define who owns, owes and controls what.</p><p>The desk lets you inspect the versions of that world: work, cash, information, control and people. The important object stays selected as the view changes.</p><button data-go="objects">Examine the ontology →</button></div><figure class="hero-scene"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 minutes of ordinary agency work with two worthwhile plans and no suspicious event. If that is dull, the case cannot rescue it.</p></div><div class="rule"><span class="num">02</span><h3>Separate the states</h3><p>Work completed, money received, a platform label, and a permission are different things. The game becomes interesting where they diverge.</p></div><div class="rule"><span class="num">03</span><h3>A story with agency</h3><p>Test an operating campus against a reconstruction park. The narrative path needs short historical anchors; the records cannot be an optional afterthought.</p></div></div><div class="pair"><div><span class="tag">Candidate A</span><h3>Run the place</h3><p>Capacity, deadlines, suppliers and commitments. Inquiry helps the player choose an operational response.</p><p class="fine">Strongest management game. Must not give historical people invented hindsight or freedom.</p></div><div><span class="tag">Candidate B</span><h3>Reconstruct the record</h3><p>Trace a chain, compare versions, distinguish explanations and identify the record that could close a question.</p><p class="fine">Strongest documentary structure. Must earn more than a sequence of inspections.</p></div></div><div class="callout"><strong>A material source finding</strong><p>The current reconstruction withdraws several vivid mechanisms used in the old explainer. Those cannot return as game mechanics. The source map records the exclusions, a citation mismatch and the limits of this review.</p><button data-go="evidence">Open the source review →</button></div><p class="fine">Read: all 58 pages of the reconstruction. <section id="objects" hidden><div class="eyebrow">02 / Ontology before scenery</div><h1>What is this thing?</h1><p class="lead">A building, company, client and payment platform can share a picture. They cannot share an identity.</p><div class="object-grid"><div class="object-menu" aria-label="Object types"><button data-object="0" aria-pressed="true">Project</button><button data-object="1" aria-pressed="false">Client</button><button data-object="2" aria-pressed="false">Employee</button><button data-object="3" aria-pressed="false">Supplier</button><button data-object="4" aria-pressed="false">Site</button><button data-object="5" aria-pressed="false">Entity</button><button data-object="6" aria-pressed="false">Obligation</button><button data-object="7" aria-pressed="false">Payment</button><button data-object="8" aria-pressed="false">Permission</button><button data-object="9" aria-pressed="false">Record</button><button data-object="10" aria-pressed="false">Claim</button></div><div class="object-detail" id="object-detail" aria-live="polite"></div></div><div class="callout"><strong>Where “Office Space” fits</strong><p>A build-menu category or content pack. Each site still needs an occupant, lessee, owner, period and gameplay purpose. Hong Kong can be both a location and a legal-entity relationship; those are separate objects.</p></div><p class="schema-note">The complete model also includes contracts, employment relationships, versioned system accounts and dated allocation links. One batch can relate to many obligations. One obligation can have several attempts. A matching amount is not an identity key.</p></section>
<section id="paths" hidden><div class="eyebrow">03 / Wireframe studies</div><h1>From a plan<br>to a useful question.</h1><p class="lead">Three paths, one coherent object model. Click through the layouts; these screens demonstrate structure, not a running economy.</p><div class="path-buttons"><button data-path="plan" aria-pressed="true"><small>PATH A / OPERATE</small>Make a campaign work</button><button data-path="payment" aria-pressed="false"><small>PATH B / RESOLVE</small>Paid, but returned</button><button data-path="compare" aria-pressed="false"><small>PATH C / RECONSTRUCT</small>Same work, changed record</button></div><div id="path-content" aria-live="polite"></div><div class="wire-note"><span class="smallcap">What this tests</span><p>Vocabulary, information order, comparison and continuity. It cannot yet establish whether management is fun, whether choices matter, or whether players understand the history. Those require the playable slice and observed testing.</p></div></section>

<section id="process" hidden><div class="eyebrow">06 / Work in the right order, with useful overlap</div><h1>Small tests.<br>Hard decisions.</h1><p class="lead">Source binding, gameplay and visual craft can proceed together. None can certify the others.</p><div class="stage-list"><div class="stage"><span class="num">01</span><div><h3>Bind one complete episode</h3><p>Obtain the current manifest, native payment chain and a legitimate control. Keep the source's assessment separate from what this team has inspected.</p></div></div><div class="stage"><span class="num">02</span><div><h3>Workshop its objects and decisions</h3><p>Source editor, systems designer, UX, art and developer follow the same job. A scene moves forward only when the nouns mean the same thing to everyone.</p></div></div><div class="stage"><span class="num">03</span><div><h3>Compare the two game structures</h3><p>Use identical information for an operating challenge and a reconstruction challenge. Watch for voluntary experimentation and accurate explanations.</p></div></div><div class="stage"><span class="num">04</span><div><h3>Build one 12–15 minute slice</h3><p>Ordinary work, one conflict, one consequential response, one valid control, one historical anchor. Enough to expose a weak loop.</p></div></div><div class="stage"><span class="num">05</span><div><h3>Choose the campaign and art pipeline</h3><p>Estimate from verified scenes, unique transitions and tested assets. A shader demo is not an art-production estimate.</p></div></div></div><div class="pair"><div><h3>Tests that can stop expansion</h3><ul class="checklist"><li>Players call every pooled payment fraud.</li><li>They add multiple representations of the same cash.</li><li>They mistake a software account for a human operator.</li><li>They cannot tell what is historical and what they changed.</li><li>The only interesting actions happen inside notices.</li></ul></div><div><h3>Open with Julia</h3><ul class="checklist"><li>First audience and intended understanding.</li><li>How far the player may change outcomes.</li><li>Current narrative authority and location of native exhibits.</li></ul><p class="fine">Until answered: assume curious outsiders with optional depth, keep both game structures open, and use invented data for interaction tests.</p></div></div><button data-go="manual">Read the complete process and test plan →</button></section>
<section id="manual" hidden><div class="doc-nav"><a href="GDD.md">Open editable Markdown ↗</a><button data-go="process">Process overview</button></div><article class="document"><h1>Agency game — design manual</h1>
<p>Revision 0.1 · 16 September 2026 · Research and design proposal</p>
<p>Working folder: Mad Money Tycoon. This is not a naming decision. The design below is a set of arguments and tests, not an approved production specification. Its factual basis and unresolved source problems are recorded in <a href="evidence-map.md">the evidence map</a>. Research reports supply the longer rationale: <a href="research/systems.md">systems</a>, <a href="research/ux.md">interface</a>, and <a href="research/visual-craft.md">visual craft</a>.</p>
<h2>01. The game worth testing</h2>
<p><strong>Build and run a place that makes things. Learn why the work happening there, the money available to it, the records describing it, and the people allowed to act can diverge.</strong></p>
<p>The agency must be a worthwhile little world before it becomes a case. Players arrange production, cultivate repeatable ways of working, manage commitments, and see people make commercials, objects, events and campaigns. A client win matters because it brings an interesting production problem, not because another logo appears on a revenue generator.</p>
<p>The disturbance is specific: a supplier is waiting although the bill says Paid; a busy studio looks much smaller in a financial report; a title changes while a permission does not; a returned transfer persists in an owner&#39;s history. These are situations to navigate. A stream of revelations with an OK button is not a management game, however good the allegations are.</p>
<p>The first design question is whether that ordinary production loop is satisfying. The second is whether inspecting conflicting records changes a decision. If either answer is no, more story will not fix it.</p>
<h3>Intended experience</h3>
<p>Competence, attachment, curiosity, pressure, and the satisfaction of finding a precise explanation. The player should sometimes improve a schedule, sometimes preserve a relationship, and sometimes have to say “the record stops here.” None of these needs a morality meter.</p>
<h3>Provisional audience and scope</h3>
<p>Until Julia answers the audience question, design for curious adults who have played a management game but do not know agency accounting, with optional depth for people close to the case. This is a working assumption. The actual court, tax and transaction materials are not yet available here, so the historical script is not ready for production.</p>
<p>Initial target: desktop browser, mouse and keyboard, pauseable single-player sessions. Test a 12–15 minute slice before estimating campaign length. A 45–90 minute authored first arc is a possibility to evaluate, not a production promise. Reading time, appetite for management, and source-review cost will determine the eventual size.</p>
<h2>02. Design commitments and open choices</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>

<p>No work in this pass changes the original references. No publication is proposed here. The browser artifact is a local design review, not the game.</p>
<h2>03. Two structures to compare</h2>
<h3>A. Operating campus</h3>
<p>The player occupies a fictional operations desk. They can plan work and make commitments within bounded authority. Their campus is expressive: project arrangements, reusable production plans, department staffing, a small number of facility choices and a few personal touches. Financial and information constraints arrive through the consequences of work.</p>
<p>On the narrative path, each tested mechanism leads into a short, required historical anchor using the same object relationships. The deeper dossier remains optional. The anchor must state what the source reports, what remains open, and what the invented run did not change; it cannot be a detachable footnote. A simulated run may change its own outcome. It cannot change the historical record or pretend to establish an unseen endpoint. Once a run diverges from a historical setup, the future must be explicitly authored fiction.</p>
<p><strong>Best at:</strong> management pleasure, care for the workforce, repeat play, familiar tycoon rhythm.</p>
<p><strong>Risk:</strong> the player may think the original people had the same information and freedom, or that getting a better score disproves the historical account. The design must distinguish actual permissions and known facts from invented challenge conditions.</p>
<h3>B. Reconstruction park</h3>
<p>The player arranges a present-day reconstruction. They activate episodes, trace relationships, compare versions and decide what a supported account can say. Space carries the history: a project, a studio, a payment desk and an off-map company become linked objects.</p>
<p><strong>Best at:</strong> the interactive-infographic ambition, deep records, honest uncertainty, source navigation.</p>
<p><strong>Risk:</strong> it becomes elaborate filing. Agency must involve discriminating explanations, choosing useful records and building intelligible comparisons, not finding the next glowing document.</p>
<h3>Comparative test</h3>
<p>Use the same returned-payment episode and the same amount of source information. In A, the immediate problem is preserving delivery while resolving settlement. In B, it is explaining what the surviving records do and do not establish. Observe which produces voluntary experimentation and accurate understanding. Do not merge both into a large campaign merely to avoid choosing.</p>
<p>Working preference: A as the playable spine, B as the linked record view, with short historical anchors inside each narrative chapter. A separate sandbox may omit them; the story path may not. Preference is not approval. The synthetic prototype can test that pairing without pretending its invented schedule happened at Madwell.</p>
<h2>04. Ontology: what things are</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Object</th>
<th>Essential relationships</th>
<th>What it becomes on screen</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Person</td>
<td>Roles and employment/engagements over time; attributed actions</td>
<td>Worker, visitor, principal or account-linked subject</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Employment</td>
<td>Employer, department, availability, compensation, benefits, period</td>
<td>Persistent park staff in creative, production, accounts, media, ops</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Supplier engagement</td>
<td>Supplier, service, milestone, rate, terms, obligation</td>
<td>Visiting specialist, crew, delivery or external service</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client relationship</td>
<td>Contracting entity and agreements</td>
<td>Client contact and a portfolio of commissions</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contract</td>
<td>Scope, fees, outside costs, approvals, rights, timing</td>
<td>Offer and terms; creates commitments, not immediate cash</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Project</td>
<td>Deliverables, dependencies, labor and supplier requirements</td>
<td>Temporary, configurable production cluster</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Site</td>
<td>Address, occupancy, facilities, lessee and lease</td>
<td>Office/studio/district with capacity and cost</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Legal entity</td>
<td>Owners, accounts, contracts, debts, rights</td>
<td>Selectable boundary; may own or occupy several sites</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Obligation</td>
<td>Debtor, creditor, currency, amount, due date, basis</td>
<td>A durable commitment, independent of a platform label</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment attempt</td>
<td>Funding, route, batch, profile, return/reissue and settlement</td>
<td>A dated lifecycle in the payment workbench</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>System account/profile</td>
<td>Platform identity, versions, privileges</td>
<td>A software/control object, not automatically a person</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission</td>
<td>Who may do what, where, when, granted by whom</td>
<td>Control edges and precise blocked-action explanations</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record/version</td>
<td>Provenance, author/account, date, immutable source and prior versions</td>
<td>Receipt, message, audit event or comparison</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Claim</td>
<td>Exact proposition, scope, supporting/conflicting records, proof boundary and review state</td>
<td>Attributed finding or open question; many records may support one claim</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Employment may be salaried, hourly or otherwise structured; “fixed cost” is a useful initial management approximation, not a claim about every actual employee. Benefits create recurring obligations rather than a generic happiness bonus. A freelance creative can appear repeatedly without becoming payroll staff.</p>
<p>Clients and vendors can share the visitor animation system. They cannot share the same economic behavior. One commissions work; the other supplies it. A courier is an engagement attached to a handoff. An artist collaboration may add a rights/licensing component as well as work. A production company may also be affiliated. Relationships carry those distinctions.</p>
<p>A freelancer&#39;s tax form is a dated reporting artifact, not an admission ticket. Form/reporting responsibility can depend on recipient and payment method. The <a href="https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-prior/i1099mec--2024.pdf">IRS&#39;s 2024 instructions</a> distinguish employment wages and card/network reporting. Historical forms must follow their own year; this reference does not establish the treatment of every case payment.</p>
<h3>Space, company and pack</h3>
<p>“Office Space” is a useful build-menu category or content collection. It is not the root data model. McKibbin, Boerum, 266 Johnson, Colorado, Porter and Hong Kong need separate answers to: what place; occupied by whom; leased or owned by which entity; when; providing what capacity; imposing what burden; relevant to which episode?</p>
<p>An affiliated company such as Starfish can participate in work, contracts and transfers while occupying the same site or remaining off-map. A property company can hold an asset without producing campaigns. Give them distinct objects even if they share an illustration.</p>
<p>Only place a site if spatial choice affects capacity, access, dependency or understandable geography. Otherwise use an off-map address. Do not build six empty districts to accommodate six names.</p>
<h2>05. Core loops and meaningful verbs</h2>
<h3>The ordinary loop</h3>
<p><strong>Accept scope → reserve capacity → commission external work → produce → obtain acceptance → invoice/collect/pay → review.</strong></p>
<p>This is a work-cycle diagram, not a cash-order rule. Deposits, media commitments, payroll and supplier payments may precede delivery, acceptance or client collection. Each step needs a visible consequence. Accepting scope reserves future time. A specialist may shorten work but require a deposit and longer notice. An internal team offers continuity but competes with another project. Acceptance unlocks a contractual milestone; it does not magically settle an invoice.</p>
<p>Three clocks drive the initial simulation: production time, commitment due dates, and cash settlement. Later record-view clocks describe posting and editing without slowing ordinary play into a bookkeeping exercise.</p>
<h3>The inquiry loop</h3>
<p><strong>Notice a consequential conflict → inspect the relevant object → compare representations → identify a useful record → narrow the explanation → adjust the plan.</strong></p>
<p>Ordinary supported transactions should be handled in batches. Inquiry attaches to an exception that affects a commitment or a historical question. The player is not employed to click 10,499 bills.</p>
<h3>The longer loop</h3>
<p>A portfolio of projects gradually increases coordination demands. Players save successful production plans, choose where to add capacity, learn which commitments tolerate delay, and recognize where authority or information blocks action. Historical chapters introduce a new distinction only after its underlying objects are familiar.</p>
<h3>What is customizable</h3>
<p>Facility mix; project placement within available space; department assignments; selected make/buy choices; schedule slack; contingency reservations; saved plan templates; desk arrangements; pinned documents; view filters; names and decoration in fictional scenarios. These choices must change something perceptible or be clearly expressive.</p>
<p>Do not simulate walking distance for payments merely because RCT has paths. A physical handoff can have a location. Bank authorization does not get faster when its building is closer to production.</p>
<h2>06. Economy and systems</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>System</th>
<th>Player input</th>
<th>Consequence</th>
<th>Hard boundary</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Capacity</td>
<td>Assign people, reserve facilities, accept scope</td>
<td>Work advances or queues</td>
<td>Work activity is not recognized revenue</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contracts</td>
<td>Choose eligible scope/production plan, seek approval</td>
<td>Rights and obligations change</td>
<td>Budget ceiling is not a cash receipt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Treasury</td>
<td>Schedule authorized payments and contingency</td>
<td>Reservations, posted bank movement and final settlement affect different liquidity measures</td>
<td>Posted cash, committed funds, usable liquidity, credit and revenue remain separate</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Allocate work, add slack, commission help</td>
<td>Availability and service continuity change</td>
<td>No invented emotional diagnosis of real people</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Suppliers</td>
<td>Commit service, approve milestones, check delivery</td>
<td>Payables and production dependencies</td>
<td>Real invoice does not close payment endpoint</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment lifecycle</td>
<td>Inspect attempts, returns, reissues, confirmation</td>
<td>An obligation may remain unresolved</td>
<td>Paid is not a universal settlement truth</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Reporting</td>
<td>Compare eligible views and periods</td>
<td>Same work can have different representations</td>
<td>A noncash reclassification cannot refill a bank</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Control</td>
<td>Inspect/request specific permissions</td>
<td>A supported action becomes available or remains blocked</td>
<td>Ownership and seniority do not grant root access</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Records</td>
<td>Preserve versions, compare sources, bind claims</td>
<td>Better explanation and retained lineage</td>
<td>Missing records do not become adverse facts by default</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Initial economy is deliberately small: one cash balance, one credit facility only if needed by the test, five department capacities, a handful of external commitments, one site. Add currencies and legal entities only when their distinctions are being taught. Hong Kong requires explicit HKD/USD fields and sourced conversion rules; a common &#39;$&#39; label is unacceptable.</p>
<p>Use integer minor units for computational money; store original precision and display rounding separately. Never round a source and later treat the rounded result as source-exact. Aggregate by declared population and economic episode. A platform object and its bank member must not become two expenses.</p>
<h3>Scoring and failure</h3>
<p>Show a small outcome panel: commitments met, production completed, cash remaining, unresolved obligations, workload strain in the fictional model, and quality of supported explanations. Do not turn them into one authoritative score until testing shows that weighting is useful and defensible.</p>
<p>A management failure can be instructive without being humiliating. Allow pause, branch, undo during planning and replay from a checkpoint. Once an interval runs, show the action history and consequence rather than a surprise penalty. Do not punish slower readers.</p>
<p>The historical closure is not a boss to beat. Discovering a system failure may be a successful research outcome while the historical company still closes. “Fraud found” is not a currency, and naming a suspect is not a high-score strategy.</p>
<h2>07. Narrative architecture</h2>
<p>The campaign should teach dependency rather than march through every date with equal weight. Its record view can always expose the full chronology.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Chapter proposal</th>
<th>First experience</th>
<th>New distinction</th>
<th>Historical anchor in CASE-01</th>
<th>Requires before script lock</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>1. The work</td>
<td>Deliver a small production using staff and a supplier</td>
<td>Work / cost / fee / receipt</td>
<td>pp.1–6, 56</td>
<td>One complete contract/job chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2. The desk</td>
<td>Responsibility without the permission to finish a task</td>
<td>Role / access / authority</td>
<td>pp.10–16</td>
<td>Dated access and correspondence exhibits</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3. The report</td>
<td>A busy campus has two different financial portraits</td>
<td>Activity / presentation</td>
<td>pp.17–22</td>
<td>Native workbooks and audience versions</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4. The payment</td>

<td>Obligation / attempt / settlement</td>
<td>pp.28–29, 33–34</td>
<td>Adverse chain plus legitimate controls</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5. The other company</td>

<tr>

</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Within each chapter: establish a normal case, let the player make a plan, introduce pressure, provide a small number of competing readings, resolve only what the records support, and return to the working place. Documents enter because they can help answer a current question.</p>
<h3>Candidate event cards</h3>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Event</th>
<th>Decision or record interaction</th>
<th>Consequence and narrative purpose</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>A commission exceeds the current workshop</td>
<td>Internal overtime/slack versus external specialist versus phased delivery</td>
<td>Establishes the attraction of growth and the cost of commitments</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client approval arrives after a reserved slot</td>
<td>Hold capacity, swap sequence, or release it</td>
<td>Makes contract time and production time diverge</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A real bill enters a pooled payment</td>
<td>Inspect batch membership or let supported routine processing proceed</td>
<td>Teaches a normal state before an exception</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A returned attempt remains labelled Paid</td>
<td>Protect delivery while seeking the right confirmation</td>
<td>Changes operations without requiring an accusation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Two transfers share amount and date</td>
<td>Compare native identities and banks</td>
<td>Defeats the tempting but invalid matching shortcut</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>An off-map affiliate requests liquidity</td>
<td>Compare authorization, return terms and remaining commitments</td>
<td>Distinguishes gross deployment from retained benefit</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A report preserves profit but changes scale</td>
<td>Pin operating activity beside two reports</td>
<td>Makes representation consequential and inspectable</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The owner cannot administer the software</td>
<td>Follow dated permission grants</td>
<td>Reveals the distance between ownership and control</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Migration keeps money fields and loses job identity</td>
<td>Restore or mark lineage using documented links</td>
<td>A puzzle about history, not “missing money” animation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Business closes but an account edits a bill</td>
<td>Compare operation date with edit date</td>
<td>Ends the productive world without inventing an evidentiary ending</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>These are proposed mechanics and record interactions, not claims that the player choices occurred. Compare, pin and follow are inspection verbs; they do not count as consequential gameplay by themselves. In operating scenarios, each must inform a commitment, schedule, contingency or supported finding. In the record view, they support an explanation without pretending to change history. Relief, tax and employee-benefit episodes are later specialist modules. They should not become simple optimization minigames until their legal/financial rules and source populations are reviewed.</p>
<h3>Characters and voice</h3>
<p>For fictional rehearsal, use characters defined by work and constraints: the producer keeping three dependencies aligned; the account lead protecting a promise; the freelancer waiting for confirmation; the operations coordinator who knows where the spare capacity is; the finance approver with a narrowly specified permission; the client contact who can approve scope but cannot accelerate treasury.</p>
<p>A person can occupy several of these roles. No sneering CFO archetype, omniscient heroic founder, or comic offshore accountant. In historical scenes, use documented roles and actions. Real-person motives and dialogue require sources. The source specifically distinguishes Rogers&#39;s authority problem from an invented fraud-suspicion motive, and named account activity from authenticated human acts.</p>
<p>Dry humor belongs in the friction of a fictional workplace and the design of objects. It does not belong in jokes about unpaid people or a made-up quote from a real participant. “No yappin” remains a source request, not dialogue to improvise.</p>
<h2>08. Interface and view system</h2>
<p>The basic arrangement is a large world with a persistent inspector, a compact view strip, a time control and a shallow desk. The computer metaphor earns its place by letting the player keep a document open while comparing the world it describes.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>View</th>
<th>Question</th>
<th>Primary marks</th>
<th>Prohibited inference</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Work</td>
<td>What is happening; what blocks it?</td>
<td>Project stages, capacity, dependencies</td>
<td>Activity equals revenue</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Cash</td>
<td>What moved and how far is it supported?</td>
<td>Bank events, attempts, returns, known endpoints</td>
<td>Paid equals settled; batch equals beneficiary</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Information</td>
<td>Which record, which version, from where?</td>
<td>Lineage, comparisons, missing links</td>
<td>Failed export equals destruction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Control</td>
<td>Who could do which action then?</td>
<td>Dated grants, approval chains, system boundaries</td>
<td>Capability proves execution</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Who supplies work and bears interruption?</td>
<td>Department commitments, employment/engagement links</td>
<td>Model strain proves a real person&#39;s feelings</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Rights and obligations remain visible in the inspector across views. Debt is not merely the negative counterpart of a cash animation. The same project remains selected, in the same place and period, when the view changes. When a historical role is represented, label event date, record version, and “available to this role at this point” separately. Evidence found later belongs to retrospective inspection and cannot silently inform an earlier decision. Document availability does not prove the person read or understood it; unknown remains unknown.</p>
<p>Every quantitative display carries measure, unit, population, period and basis. Necessary qualifiers are visible without hovering. The detailed source locator can be one step away. Separate zero, not applicable, unavailable, not supplied, not yet inspected and modeled.</p>
<h3>Three desk arrangements</h3>
<ol>
<li><strong>World + inspector:</strong> plan and watch activity; one selected object and its practical next actions.</li>
<li><strong>Compare:</strong> two records plus the same object&#39;s context, with retained and changed fields marked separately.</li>
<li><strong>Read:</strong> comfortable long-form text with a persistent return target, source provenance and personal notes.</li>
</ol>
<p>Do not implement a literal unrestricted window manager. Test two pinned comparisons and one active inspector before adding more. An infinite pile of windows would faithfully reproduce office work. That is not a recommendation.</p>
<h3>Payment workbench</h3>
<p>Three columns: obligation and terms; bank/processor lifecycle; platform label and version. Connect them through stable IDs. A finance building can open this workbench and represent staff review capacity. Every bill or project also opens it directly. Permissions are enforced by the model, not by walking to a kiosk.</p>
<p>The first wireframes and three detailed paths are in <a href="research/ux.md">the interface research</a>. The local design review contains a compact clickable version: it is a layout/semantics study with invented data, not a banking simulation.</p>
<h3>Accessibility and continuity</h3>
<p>Keyboard equivalent for every action; visible focus; nonspatial object list; independent text scaling; labels plus patterns for status; pause while reading; reduced motion; adjustable sound; no hover-only evidence; reliable back/navigation history. At small widths, show world or inspector with explicit return instead of squeezing both into illegibility. Mobile is a reading/review target initially, not a promised full management-control target.</p>
<h2>09. Visual direction and production craft</h2>
<p>The world and the desk need different resolutions. Small pre-rendered people, machinery and buildings can retain RCT&#39;s legibility. Documents, amounts and controls should remain crisp at normal reading size. “8-bit” is not an art specification.</p>
<p>The three researched directions are evaluated in <a href="research/visual-craft.md">visual craft</a> and the accompanying style tiles. Initial preference is a warm institutional campus: compact production buildings, confident signs, olive/ink/paper surfaces, selective bright equipment, and restrained physical depth in the desk. The alternatives test a print-production character and a cooler systems-oriented atmosphere. The same source states must remain legible under all three.</p>
<p><strong>Reference extraction:</strong> RCT supplies a place that can be watched and diagnosed; Poolside.fm suggests a designed instrument with personality; institutional corporate design supplies hierarchy; Project Zomboid supplies material specificity, persistent state and mod-aware craft; Mike Wilks supplies sectional glimpses of infrastructure. None is a skin to apply wholesale. The local garden supplies an especially useful precedent: define a semantic field once, then let multiple renderings expose different properties.</p>
<h3>Proposed pipeline test</h3>
<p>Model one small studio, one production module, a person and a visiting supplier. Render fixed orthographic views with a common sun, ground plane, pivot and pixel scale. Bake a color pass and, only if useful, depth/normal/selection masks. Compare a plain atlas with selective depth-aware shading. Retouch silhouettes and critical edges by hand.</p>
<p>Start with a 2:1 screen-grid study, a fixed camera, four orientations only where rotation has gameplay value, and two integer zoom levels. These are proposed constraints to test, not claims about original RCT technical parameters. Lighting must not obscure hit targets or status marks. Dither belongs on world materials or shadows if it improves the image; never apply it to financial digits or source text.</p>
<p>Compare that pipeline against simple live orthographic 3D using the same scene. Evaluate readability, art iteration time, occlusion, file size, performance and the cost of producing the next twenty objects. Choose the cheapest pipeline that preserves the intended image. Do not build a shader system before discovering whether the player&#39;s main view needs it.</p>
<h3>Asset procurement and rights</h3>
<p>The linked PZ mods are craft references with their own restrictions. Their availability in a workshop does not grant reuse in this game. Use licensed general-purpose assets only as a blockout or explicitly cleared base; record author, version, license, attribution, redistribution and modification terms. Avoid a mixed pack look by matching scale, bevels, silhouettes, lighting and texture frequency before adding content.</p>
<p>No RCT sprites, PZ tiles, third-party logos, screenshots or source-document images become production assets by default. The research report distinguishes what was inspected from what is proposed. Any final real-name/reference strategy belongs to the distribution decision.</p>
<h2>10. Sound, motion and atmosphere</h2>
<p>Build a small sound vocabulary around work: fabrication rhythm, studio hum, footsteps, deliveries, paper handling and restrained system feedback. The world should sound productive before it sounds tense. Use short state-change cues; repeated notices must not become alarms. Pause or soften the world when a long record is open.</p>
<p>Animation communicates transitions: a dependency handed off, a queue thinning, a room going idle, a permission request pending. Cash-flow pulses require an observed/simulated transfer with declared status; information edges use a visibly different motion. Unknown endpoints end visibly. They do not disappear into cinematic fog.</p>
<p>Music is optional until the loop works. No casino soundtrack, “sinister finance” sting or escalating dread tied to an unproved inference. Tension can come from a deadline and a quiet machine.</p>
<h2>11. The first 15 minutes to prototype</h2>
<p>Everything in this scenario is invented. The actual case comparison is separately labelled and fixed.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Time budget to test</th>
<th>Player activity</th>
<th>What we learn from the test</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>0–3 minutes</td>
<td>Review a $100,000 campaign, choose a production plan, place its cluster and reserve departments</td>
<td>Can the player form an intention without reading a manual?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3–6</td>
<td>Run an interval; resolve an ordinary capacity conflict; watch a deliverable progress</td>
<td>Is the management enjoyable with no anomaly?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>6–9</td>
<td>Supplier component waits; inspect a $12,000 obligation labelled Paid; find debit and equal return</td>
<td>Can the player distinguish label, attempt and settlement?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>9–12</td>
<td>Reserve substitute capacity, defer a handoff or revise sequence while requesting useful confirmation</td>
<td>Does inquiry change an operating decision?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>12–15</td>
<td>Compare a legitimate pooled payment, review the run, then examine a short source-bound historical anchor</td>
<td>Can the player transfer the distinction without calling everything fraud?</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Confirmation can remain pending. The player need not solve the whole payment history to make a sensible contingency plan. An unrelated record request should explain its limited use instead of returning “wrong answer.”</p>
<p>The source-backed historical analogue is Creative Peaks on p.34, paired with the valid pooling controls on pp.45–46. The narrative-path transfer check asks the player to distinguish what their run changed from what the historical records establish. If that handoff feels like homework bolted onto the game, revise the chapter seam before proceeding. It is not ready for a certified historical scene until its native chain is inspected.</p>
<h2>12. Technical architecture to support the design</h2>
<p>Do not select a full engine during this research pass. The first prototype can use a modest canvas/SVG scene and DOM interface. Decide between a dedicated 2D engine and a renderer only after measuring scene complexity and art requirements.</p>
<p>Keep five components independent:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Source archive and claims:</strong> immutable input snapshots, locators, corrections and review states.</li>
<li><strong>Historical objects and events:</strong> source-bound identities, relationships, versions and uncertainty.</li>
<li><strong>Scenario model:</strong> invented parameters, allowed actions, seeded events, outcome rules and replay history.</li>
<li><strong>Presentation:</strong> world sprites, view projections, document rendering and accessible lists.</li>
<li><strong>Review tools:</strong> claim coverage, changed-source impact, source-to-scene links and exportable comparisons.</li>
</ol>
<p>An event has an economic identity and a representation identity. Returns reference the attempt they reverse. Reissues reference the obligation they try to settle. Later edits reference prior record versions. Use typed, sourced many-to-many allocation links: one batch can support several obligations, one obligation can have partial settlements and several attempts, and one record can span multiple clients. Do not invent fractional client allocations for unsplit multi-client objects, or treat shared journal context as transaction identity. Use effective time and recorded time rather than overwriting a row. A source correction must invalidate dependent narrative copy and diagrams visibly.</p>
<p>A save records scenario version, data/source version, random seed, decisions, pins, layout and view state. Fictional parameters never write into the historical dataset. An export identifies its selected population and basis; it does not fabricate a grand total from overlapping surfaces.</p>
<h3>Modding and expansion</h3>
<p>Plan a small data boundary now: art packs; fictional scenario packs; independently versioned document/source packs. A mod manifest declares compatible model version, dependencies, license and whether data are historical or invented. Test mods in a separate save namespace. Loading a mod must never overwrite provenance or make invented content look source-certified.</p>
<p>Do not promise Steam Workshop or unrestricted scripting for a browser prototype. JSON scenarios and replaceable atlases may be enough. The useful lesson from tile mods is reusable components with known dependencies, not an early commitment to a distribution platform.</p>
<h2>13. Accuracy process</h2>
<p>Zero error is a standard to work toward, not a claim that follows from careful prose. The reconstruction contains a source-route mismatch and differences that require native records. This pass identifies them; it does not certify the whole case.</p>
<p>For each historical scene, complete one record: proposition; event and record dates; source and exact locator; amount/currency/population; supporting and conflicting evidence; endpoint boundary; source&#39;s assessment; our inspection status; withdrawn predecessors; player action; simulated assumptions; and the argument the mechanic teaches.</p>
<p>Checks must include arithmetic and meaning. A number can be copied perfectly into the wrong denominator. A quote can be verbatim and still omit its resolution. A same-date/same-amount join can connect two distinct transfers. The current PDF itself includes those warnings.</p>
<p>Require three reviews at each historical scene gate: source/financial interpretation, gameplay comprehension, and interface semantics. An AI research agent&#39;s review is preparatory work; it does not substitute for the relevant human/source authority. Disagreement is recorded beside the scene, with the unresolved claim left inactive rather than quietly resolved for narrative convenience.</p>
<h2>14. Production plan and decision gates</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Phase</th>
<th>Work and concrete output</th>
<th>Proceed only when</th>
<th>Work that can run alongside it</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>0. Source orientation — this pass</td>
<td>Corpus inventory, full chapter coverage, 48-technique routing, corrections, draft GDD, research and style options</td>
<td>Julia can see the assumptions and challenge the architecture</td>
<td>Public precedent research and art-pipeline comparison</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1. Source binding</td>
<td>Acquire manifests and first episode&#39;s native exhibits; build claims and lifecycle records</td>
<td>No unresolved numeric/identity claim is needed by that episode</td>
<td>Synthetic capacity prototype; sprite readability study</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2. Ontology workshop</td>
<td>Map a contract, site, entity, employee, supplier, obligation, payment and record through one complete job</td>
<td>Same object can survive all views without changing meaning</td>
<td>Two competing player-role storyboards</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3. Paper/clickable playtest</td>
<td>Test operating-campus and reconstruction-park versions of one episode</td>
<td>Players can choose and explain consequences; ordinary play has appeal</td>
<td>Art style tiles and document legibility tests</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4. Thin playable slice</td>
<td>12–15 minute loop, limited placeable objects, one conflict and one valid control</td>
<td>No systematic confusion about money/state/provenance; players voluntarily try a second plan</td>
<td>Source work on next chapter; pipeline cost benchmark</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5. Campaign outline lock</td>
<td>Sequence learning prerequisites, historical anchors, permissions, events and ending</td>
<td>Each chapter earns a new distinction and has source coverage</td>
<td>Additional art and sound studies only for approved objects</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>6. Production</td>
<td>Author episodes, assets and software against the chosen model</td>
<td>Each scene passes semantic and source review</td>
<td>Accessibility, saves, performance, mod boundary</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>7. Release preparation</td>
<td>Audience-specific editing, attribution, rights, packaging and distribution checks</td>
<td>Known review findings resolved; version and source scope explicit</td>
<td>Optional read-only infographic export</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Do not attach calendar estimates to unknown exhibit volume. After the first source-bound slice, estimate by verified scene count, unique state transitions, unique assets and review effort. Repeated mechanics are cheap; repeated source adjudication may not be.</p>
<h3>Division of work</h3>
<p>The source editor maintains claims, corrections and narrative coverage. The systems designer owns actions, state transitions and incentives. The UX designer owns continuity, disclosure, comparison and accessible paths. The art/technical-art designer owns scale, camera, asset grammar and readable states. The developer implements the agreed model and replay tools. One integrator checks that all five are still describing the same object.</p>
<p>Workshop each scene with all relevant roles before producing it: source editor explains the exact distinction; systems proposes two decisions; UX draws the path; art identifies what can be shown without false inference; developer identifies hidden state and cost. Then deliberately test a legitimate counterexample. This is the useful design meeting; a moodboard presentation alone will not do it.</p>
<h2>15. Test plan</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Test</th>
<th>Prompt / setup</th>
<th>Failure means</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Ordinary play</td>
<td>Three minutes of production with no anomaly and two viable plans</td>
<td>If boring, revise management before adding investigation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>State discrimination</td>
<td>Paid/settled, returned/Paid, valid pooling, duplicate records, unknown endpoint</td>
<td>Any recurring false inference stops content expansion</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>View continuity</td>
<td>Follow one obligation through five views and return</td>
<td>Lost identity/time requires interface redesign</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission model</td>
<td>Owner lacks export; operator can initiate but not approve</td>
<td>If title substitutes for authority, model or UI is wrong</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Nonadditivity</td>
<td>Show bill, batch member, ledger and bank event</td>
<td>Counting one episode twice is a release blocker</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>History versus fiction</td>
<td>Ask what the player changed and what actually happened</td>
<td>Blurred answers require stronger separation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Recall</td>
<td>Next day: sketch work→obligation→attempt→settlement→record</td>
<td>Memorized accusations without usable distinctions is failure</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Art readability</td>
<td>Identify roles, selection and blocked work at two zooms</td>
<td>If style destroys legibility, adjust assets before enlarging world</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Artifact QA</td>
<td>Keyboard, scaling, narrow layout, reduced motion, save/reopen</td>
<td>Navigation and reading must remain reliable</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Start with six to eight participants spanning management-game and financial familiarity. These are diagnostic sessions, not statistical validation. Record behavior and mistaken explanations, not just whether participants liked the look. Keep the instructions short enough that they do not teach the answer immediately before testing it.</p>
<h2>16. Why these precedents matter</h2>
<p>RCT contributes diagnosable local systems, meaningful scenario constraints and reusable construction. Doug Church&#39;s 1999 design tools provide intention and visible consequence. MDA links a rule to the behavior and experience it produces. Jenkins supplies spatial narrative beyond cutscenes. Period HCI supplies direct manipulation, comparison and organized windows. Each is applied to a concrete choice in the research reports, not cited as a style badge.</p>
<p>A useful counterweight is Miguel Sicart&#39;s <a href="https://gamestudies.org/1103/articles/sicart_ap">Against Procedurality</a>: a designer&#39;s rules do not exhaust what play means. Here that means preserving expression, alternative plans and the player&#39;s ability to question a model. A game that rewards agreement with an author has a very efficient plot and very little inquiry. This is a design inference from the critique, not an empirical claim about what this game will teach.</p>
<h2>17. Decisions for the next workshop</h2>
<ol>
<li>Who is the first audience, and what should they be able to explain afterward?</li>
<li>What can the player change: operations in invented scenarios, discovery in the historical record, or an explicit alternative history?</li>
<li>Is CASE-01 the current narrative authority, and where are its native exhibits and current correction manifest?</li>
<li>Which three objects deserve to be enjoyable before any case material appears?</li>
<li>Which ordinary, legitimate transaction will sit beside the first adverse example?</li>
<li>Which visual direction makes the same scene easiest to understand and worth watching?</li>
</ol>
<p>The first three questions are with Julia. The remaining three can be worked through using the research and review artifact now.</p>

<h2>Non-negotiable distinctions in the model</h2>
<ol>
<li><strong>1072 is not a bank.</strong> It is a synthetic clearing account. A spatial service building may provide access to payment work; it must not store the modeled $141.578m field as cash.</li>
<li><strong>An economic episode has several representations.</strong> A bill, platform Paid object, journal, bank debit and tax record may describe related aspects of one episode. Summing the screens can count the same event repeatedly.</li>
<li><strong>Dates have jobs.</strong> Service date, invoice date, posting date, cash-settlement date, later edit date, and the date a participant could know something need separate fields.</li>
<li><strong>The endpoint can be unknown.</strong> A bank batch can be observed while its final recipient is unclosed. Unknown is neither zero nor stolen nor legitimate.</li>
<li><strong>An employee is not a row called Employee.</strong> Nicole Brunner&#39;s surviving imported label does not establish her presence or later actions (p.36).</li>
<li><strong>An account label is not a physical user.</strong> Import Administration is not a character. Named platform activity needs distinct attribution from authenticated human action (pp.35–39, 47).</li>
<li><strong>The $141.578m and $141.749m fields overlap.</strong> Different period windows and different measurement bases are explicit. Their near agreement cannot animate a dollar-by-dollar trail or become additive damages (pp.8–9, 46–47).</li>
<li><strong>2024 modeled input is unavailable.</strong> The computational zero used in the discharge model is not evidence of no business activity (p.9).</li>
<li><strong>A contract maximum is not revenue.</strong> Fees, estimated outside costs, paid amounts and recognized income are separate. Components already included in a budget are not added again (p.56).</li>
<li><strong>The appendix&#39;s endpoint grades are not a guilt scale.</strong> They describe capability, records, intermediate cash and increasingly specific endpoints; entitlement and purpose still matter (p.47).</li>
</ol>
<h2>Issues found during this pass</h2>

<p>All 48 have a proposed home. That is coverage, not verification and not a commitment to 48 mechanics. A small number of reusable state transitions should explain many techniques.</p>
<h2>Claim record required before historical content ships</h2>
<p>Each claim needs an ID; exact proposition; source hash; document/page or native row/object locator; quoted/paraphrased status; currency/unit; amount precision; period; economic episode ID; population/grain; event date; record date; record-availability date, documented receipt/read date, and any separately labelled inference about knowledge (each may be unknown); endpoint; current proof boundary; supporting and conflicting sources; superseded-by link; and human review status. A mechanic adds learning objective, player verb, fabricated parameters, and failure cases.</p>
<p>Store the source&#39;s assessment separately from our review status. “Source calls this record-closed / exhibit not yet inspected” is a coherent state. “Verified” without saying what was checked is not.</p>
<p>The first five acquisitions are: current release manifest and corrections package; Creative Peaks/Reddit plus legitimate pooled controls; 2018 labor/access correspondence; contract/job/client lineage for one complete production; and the current object/settlement data underlying any aggregate view. The ERC workbooks, office/site records, “no yappin” message and tax/benefits details follow their prospective scenes. They should not hold up synthetic tests of basic gameplay.</p>
</article></section>
<section id="research" hidden><div class="eyebrow">09 / Independent research, then a shared review</div><h1>Arguments behind<br>the design.</h1><label for="research-picker" class="smallcap">Research discipline</label><br><select id="research-picker" class="research-picker"><option value="systems">Game systems and precedents</option><option value="ux">Interface, paths and period HCI</option><option value="visual">Visual craft, pipelines and references</option></select><article class="document research-doc" id="research-systems"><h1>Systems research and ontology — working argument</h1>
<p>Research date: 16 September 2026. Status: design proposals for discussion, not an approved game specification.</p>
<h2>The structural decision</h2>
<p>Build the model around <strong>work, obligations, records, and authority</strong>. Let the park make those relationships tangible. A client, a building, an affiliated company, and a payment platform cannot all become rides without losing precisely the distinctions the story depends on.</p>
<p>The promising player experience is running a productive place while discovering that its reports, payments, and permissions describe different versions of that place. Production must be satisfying before the discrepancies arrive. Otherwise we have a document viewer with pedestrians.</p>
<p>The source reviewed is the complete 58-page <em>The Zero Machine</em>, September 2, 2026, through its extracted text in <code>Design/working/case-text.txt</code>. It is a counsel briefing and an argumentative synthesis. Its underlying bank files, platform exports, correspondence, workbooks, and claim-level manifests have not been independently inspected here. Throughout this report, <strong>briefing reports</strong> means that document makes the claim; <strong>design inference</strong> means a proposed interpretation; <strong>design fiction</strong> means invented playable conditions. The source&#39;s demands about governing verbs and future conclusions are content to assess, not instructions to this design team.</p>
<h2>What the source makes structurally necessary</h2>
<p>Four states must remain separate:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Operating reality:</strong> work performed, staff time, supplier commitments, delivery, client acceptance.</li>
<li><strong>Economic rights and burdens:</strong> who owes whom, contractual entitlement, ownership, debt, guarantees.</li>
<li><strong>Represented state:</strong> what a platform, journal, report, tax package, or named account says happened.</li>
<li><strong>External movement and control:</strong> bank settlement, last supported recipient, and who could view, initiate, approve, alter, administer, or recover each system.</li>
</ol>
<p>Time cuts across all four. Work date, due date, attempted payment date, settlement date, posting date, edit date, reporting period, and discovery date are different fields. A single timeline slider without a declared clock will quietly falsify the story.</p>
<p>The briefing&#39;s integrated-production argument is foundational: an outside purchase at cost and work performed inside the enterprise can look alike in a payment list while having different economics (pp. 1, 5–6, 45, 56). This argues for <strong>make/buy/affiliate relationships</strong>, not “expense type = suspicious.” The exact accounting treatment of an actual engagement still requires its contract and relevant analysis.</p>
<p>Account 1072 is a synthetic clearing account, not a bank or a place where segregated money sat (pp. 1, 8–9, 44). A glowing reservoir building would make the metaphor look physically true. The modeled carried field belongs in an explicitly labeled analytical view; the bank balance belongs in treasury. The $141.578 million statement difference and $141.749 million release measure overlap and have different observation periods. They must never become two piles of stolen money. The quarterly field is partly allocated, and its exact path is model-dependent.</p>
<p>The source also supplies counterexamples. Ten ordinary pooled allocations total $1.014 million; 65 intercompany pairs have bank support; the SiriusXM $150,000 payment is genuine; a vendor merge previously treated as a conflict is resolved (pp. 45–46). The early Malaky accommodation is not established as fraudulent at inception. Withdrawn cap/penny, hidden-card, and complete refund-loop theories must stay withdrawn (p. 44). A sound mechanic must accommodate these cases without special pleading.</p>
<h2>Entity taxonomy</h2>
<p>Use stable identities with dated relationships. A person can be an employee, owner, authorized signer, and account holder at different times. One label should not erase the others.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Object</th>
<th>What it owns</th>
<th>Playable expression</th>
<th>Do not collapse into</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Person</td>
<td>Dated roles, documented actions, relationships</td>
<td>Named character or anonymized worker; inspector</td>
<td>Platform username or inferred motive</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Employment relationship</td>
<td>Employer, department, capacity, recurring compensation and benefits</td>
<td>Persistent park staff grouped as creative, production, accounts, media, ops</td>
<td>Universal salary model or generic vendor</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Supplier engagement</td>
<td>Supplier, service, milestones, rates, invoice terms</td>
<td>Visiting crew, courier, specialist, external delivery</td>
<td>A guest buying entertainment</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client relationship</td>
<td>Contracting party and portfolio of agreements</td>
<td>Client representative and commission board</td>
<td>A permanently profitable attraction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contract / commission</td>
<td>Scope, approvals, fee, outside costs, rights, deadlines</td>
<td>Job offered to the agency</td>
<td>Cash already received</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Project / work package</td>
<td>Tasks, dependencies, capacity reservations, deliverables</td>
<td>Placeable production cluster, changing with the job</td>
<td>Legal entity or client identity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Site / lease / facility</td>
<td>Physical capacity, lease terms, occupancy dates</td>
<td>Office, studio, fabrication area, satellite site</td>
<td>Property owner or affiliate</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Legal entity</td>
<td>Ownership, accounts, obligations, relationships</td>
<td>Selectable boundary or district; sometimes off-map</td>
<td>Building or decorative brand</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Obligation</td>
<td>Debtor, creditor, amount, currency, basis, due date</td>
<td>Persistent promise that can remain unresolved</td>
<td>Invoice image or Paid label</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment attempt / settlement</td>
<td>Funding, batch membership, method, returns, endpoint evidence</td>
<td>Payment workbench and lifecycle trace</td>
<td>Obligation itself</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Account / platform profile</td>
<td>System identity, versions, linked credentials and permissions</td>
<td>Desktop application and control inspector</td>
<td>Natural person at keyboard</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record / version / claim</td>
<td>Source locator, authoring context, time, scope, contradiction, proof limit</td>
<td>Inspectable receipt, message, report, comparison</td>
<td>Ground truth</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission grant</td>
<td>Holder, target, action, start/end, delegator</td>
<td>Control overlay and blocked-action explanation</td>
<td>Job title or ownership percentage</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Clients and suppliers may both appear as visitors in the scene, but they need different behaviors and economic relations. A freelancer can be a recurring character and still remain a supplier engagement. A payroll employee remains a person when their work is allocated to an affiliate.</p>
<p>The suggested attached 1099 is useful as a discoverable record, not a species marker. Reporting depends on the relationship, payment type, recipient, and year. The IRS&#39;s 2024 instructions explicitly distinguish wages and card/network reporting. Model a reporting profile and dated form record; do not stamp every freelancer “1099” at entry. This also preserves the case&#39;s alleged changes in tax identity and reporting route. <a href="https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-prior/i1099mec--2024.pdf">IRS 2024 instructions</a>.</p>
<p>“Office Space” can be a build-menu category. It should not be an ontology. McKibbin, Boerum, Johnson, Colorado, Porter, and Hong Kong require individual source checks before entering the scenario: physical location, lessee, operator, legal entity, opening period, and actual narrative function. Hong Kong already demonstrates why site and entity must separate: the briefing describes both local production and intercompany cash/accounting (pp. 2, 22, 48).</p>
<h2>Two viable architectures</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th></th>
<th>A. Operating campus</th>
<th>B. Reconstruction park</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Player role</td>
<td>Fictional operations desk with bounded authority</td>
<td>Present-day investigator arranging a reconstruction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Main verb</td>
<td>Plan and deliver work</td>
<td>Reconstruct and test an explanation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Spatial model</td>
<td>Facilities and production capacity; project clusters</td>
<td>Stable historical sites with reconstructed work and transaction paths</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Decisions</td>
<td>Capacity, sequencing, commitments, contingency, information requests</td>
<td>Which chain to examine, which record would distinguish explanations, how to present a bounded finding</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Story delivery</td>
<td>Ordinary work develops contradictions; case records appear at relevant mechanisms</td>
<td>Historical episodes become traversable systems; records open from their objects</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Strength</td>
<td>Familiar management pleasure and consequences before exposition</td>
<td>Stronger fidelity; unresolved endpoints can stay unresolved</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Risk</td>
<td>Counterfactual choices may imply that real people could simply have prevented the outcome</td>
<td>Investigation can become matching receipts until a chapter unlocks</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Scope</td>
<td>Small authored scenario plus reusable simulation</td>
<td>Larger evidence apparatus, lighter operational simulation</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p><strong>Recommendation: test A and B with the same small episode before choosing the campaign.</strong> A is the stronger candidate for the requested game; B is the stronger reference and explainer mode. They can share objects without pretending to share outcomes.</p>
<p>In A, explicitly fictional training or alternate scenarios allow operational choices to change results. Historical case records remain fixed and inspectable. In B, agency changes what the player understands and what they can substantiate; it does not rewrite a real bank event. Avoid a historical avatar endowed with every password and hindsight. The briefing&#39;s unequal access is not a difficulty setting that a clever player should bypass.</p>
<p>A literal “client rides, finance kiosks, fraud meter” architecture is a weak third option. It is initially recognizable but turns source categories into arbitrary gameplay categories. A kiosk can represent a physical service counter if space and staffing matter. BILL is a workbench because its consequential properties are profiles, batches, states, permissions, and evidence—not its walking distance from the studio.</p>
<h2>RCT craft worth carrying across</h2>
<p>RCT&#39;s useful inheritance is <strong>diagnosable systems assembled into a place</strong>. Keep the loop of placing a capability, watching demand and throughput, inspecting a local failure, and making a bounded adjustment.</p>
<p>There is a precise historical example of a scenario rule changing the whole economic problem: Sawyer says the new <em>Loopy Landscapes</em> parks deliberately prevent raising admission, making individual attractions earn their income. Transfer that principle to different agency contracts: a fixed retainer, approval-dependent production budget, or media commitment should change what good planning means. Do not treat all incoming money as the same fare. <a href="https://www.chrissawyergames.com/faq1.htm">Sawyer FAQ</a>.</p>
<p>OpenRCT2&#39;s guest implementation exposes needs, preferences, satisfaction, affordability-related behavior, and local thoughts; its ride-ratings implementation evaluates track context and proximity. These are useful inspectable mechanisms in a modern reconstruction, not proof that every current behavior exactly matches the 1999 executable. Transfer the causal specificity: “waiting for supplier confirmation” should point to a real dependency; “stressed” should not be the only diagnosis. A beautiful campus may still contain an impossible schedule. <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2/develop/src/openrct2/entity/Guest.cpp">Guest source</a>, <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2/develop/src/openrct2/ride/RideRatings.cpp">ratings source</a>.</p>
<p>Borrow prebuilt and editable <strong>production plans</strong>: a repeatable campaign package is analogous to a reusable ride design. The player can customize a few consequential parts—make/buy, staffing, sequence, contingency, milestones—without laying every metre of track. Sawyer&#39;s own downloadable track collection is a period example of designed objects being shared and tested in existing scenarios. <a href="https://www.chrissawyergames.com/download1.htm">Track collection</a>.</p>
<p>Do not import pathological incentives accidentally. If the score is revenue, the player should chase revenue even when that destroys the intended lesson. If finding fraud earns points, every ordinary payment becomes a missed scoring opportunity. Use separate outcomes: commitments honored, work delivered, liquidity maintained, people burdened, records preserved, claims supported. A bad business outcome may coexist with excellent reasoning. No overall righteousness percentage is required.</p>
<p>The user-supplied <a href="https://drew1440.com/2024/09/24/rollercoaster-tycoon/">Drew1440 RCT page</a> is a secondary visual/reference lead. The retrieved text is sparse; it has not been used to establish mechanics.</p>
<h2>SimCity, Zomboid, and period design theory</h2>
<p>SimCity designer Guillaume Pierre describes maps, networks/globals, agents, and building-to-building connections as different simulation techniques. That distinction is useful here: floor capacity belongs to space; obligations form a network; worker availability can be aggregated; an individual courier need only be simulated if their behavior changes a decision. There is no prize for giving every invoice pathfinding. This is an inference from his published GDC abstract, not a claim to have reviewed the complete lecture. <a href="https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1034347/We-Built-This-City-on">GDC 2024</a>.</p>
<p>Project Zomboid offers a more useful precedent than zombies: domestic objects, persistent consequences, player-made plans, and adjustable scenario conditions. Its 2013 developer post explicitly separates canonical survival rules from customizable sandbox rules. That supports a historical record mode and a clearly marked experiment mode. It does not justify making documented uncertainty a random dice roll. <a href="https://projectzomboid.com/blog/news/2013/04/in-the-guts-of-the-sandbox/">The Indie Stone, 2013</a>.</p>
<p>Three period/near-period tools should govern the GDD:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Doug Church, 1999:</strong> intention and perceivable consequence. Before implementing a choice, write the plan a player could form and the visible consequence that would teach them whether it worked. “Click OK to continue” satisfies neither. <a href="https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/formal-abstract-design-tools">Formal Abstract Design Tools</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Hunicke, LeBlanc, Zubek, 2004:</strong> use mechanics/dynamics/aesthetics as a testable chain. A capacity reservation is a mechanic; juggling competing commitments is the dynamic; capable stewardship under pressure is the intended experience. Extra documents do not automatically improve that chain. <a href="https://aaai.org/papers/ws04-04-001-mda-a-formal-approach-to-game-design-and-game-research/">MDA paper</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Henry Jenkins, 2004:</strong> embed narrative information in the environment and its objects. An idle fabrication area beside a report that claims costs vanished can create an inquiry; a popup explaining that contradiction cannot substitute for it. This is our application of narrative architecture, not an assertion about the case. <a href="https://electronicbookreview.com/publications/game-design-as-narrative-architecture/">Game Design as Narrative Architecture</a>.</li>
</ul>
<p>Bogost&#39;s procedural rhetoric adds the necessary criticism: rules themselves make arguments. Making every clearing transaction harmful would teach that clearing is inherently fraudulent, whatever the footnote says. Elizabeth Sampat&#39;s GDC abstract similarly treats simulation design as an expression of assumptions. The GDD needs a short <strong>argument made by this rule</strong> field and a counterexample beside each financial mechanism. <a href="https://bogost.com/books/persuasive-games">Bogost</a>, <a href="https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1023994/Systems-Make-Statements-Simulations-and">Sampat</a>.</p>
<p>Stone Librande&#39;s <em>Simulating a City, One Page at a Time</em> is a useful documentation precedent: a large design needs inspectable subsystem pages. Use a comprehensive GDD index plus one page per loop, state machine, and source-to-mechanic mapping; do not attempt to make every detail live in one immaculate monolith. Only the published session abstract was reviewed. <a href="https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1018764/Simulating-a-City-One-Page">GDC 2013</a>.</p>
<h2>The playable loop and the first workshop</h2>
<p>The ordinary loop is <strong>accept scope → reserve capacity → commit suppliers → produce → obtain acceptance → collect/pay → review</strong>. Capacity, cash, and settlement move on different clocks. Departments should have skills and finite time; payroll and benefits recur independently of client collection. External engagements add flexible capacity but also lead times, milestones, approval conditions, and obligations. Neither should be universally cheaper or morally preferred.</p>
<p>The inquiry loop interrupts only when it changes a decision: <strong>notice conflict → compare representations → name the missing evidence → request or inspect it → narrow the finding → revise an operating plan</strong>. Do not require every bill to become a puzzle. Batch ordinary supported work and surface meaningful exceptions.</p>
<p>Workshop agreed with the UX research agent, entirely design fiction: Campaign C-07 has a $100,000 budget, an internal team, and a fabrication supplier. A $12,000 obligation is marked Paid; the bank shows a debit and an equal return; no subsequent settlement has been observed. A deadline approaches. The player can reserve substitute capacity, protect another project&#39;s slot, request supplier confirmation, and continue with contingency or defer the handoff. The confirmation may be pending or unavailable: incomplete observation does not establish nonpayment.</p>
<p>The interesting decision is spending scarce capacity or cash to keep a promise while resolving uncertainty. The contradiction is not a multiple-choice accusation. A normal pooled-payment example accompanies it. Later, the actual Creative Peaks record can be inspected as “briefing cites this chain; underlying exhibits pending,” preserving the source distinction (p. 34).</p>
