<h2>Discovery order and views</h2>
<p>Introduce <strong>one job</strong>, then <strong>one payment lifecycle</strong>, then <strong>multiple representations</strong>, then <strong>permissions</strong>, then <strong>affiliates and burden</strong>, then <strong>migration and afterlife</strong>. This is a learning order, not a rewritten chronology. Each later case episode retains its actual dates. A 1990s desktop presentation is an interface convention; it must not move a later chat, bank product, or AI conversation into the 1990s.</p>
<p>Offer four coordinated views of the same selection: <strong>Work</strong> (capacity/dependencies), <strong>Money</strong> (settled movement and obligations, distinct), <strong>Control</strong> (dated permissions), <strong>Record</strong> (versions, sources, claims). Keep the selected project/person/obligation pinned while switching. An additional comparison shows the company as presented to a specified audience. “Reported sales” must name its report and period.</p>
<p>Real named characters need source-based roles and documented acts. Use invented archetypes only in the fictional scenario: the producer protecting a deadline, the specialist waiting on a deposit, the account lead selling tomorrow&#39;s capacity. Their behaviors should have mundane operational causes. A named person&#39;s alleged culpability is not an NPC trait.</p>
<h2>Mods: borrow the architecture, check the asset</h2>
<p>The linked Nexus resource is <strong>Item Icons — Modders Resource</strong>, by Incariuz: 341 custom icons plus four templates. Its stated permission confines use to Project Zomboid mods, and some assets modify the game&#39;s own art. It is a craft reference, not a ready asset pack for this standalone game. <a href="https://www.nexusmods.com/projectzomboid/mods/37">Author page</a>.</p>
<p>The linked Workshop item is <strong>Daddy Dirkie dirks tiles</strong>. It functions as a dependency for maps rather than changing the standalone game itself. The description allows credited map use but prohibits redistribution/editing without permission; later author comments discuss community continuation, which is not a clear standalone-game license. Treat it as a reference for coherent environmental kits and dependency design. <a href="https://steamcommunity.com/workshop/filedetails/?id=2337452747">Workshop author listing</a>.</p>
<p>Plan separately for visual packs, fictional scenario packs, and rule experiments. Historical evidence packs need source/version identity and must not be silently overwritten by a mod. OpenRCT2&#39;s documented script/plugin extension is a useful example of adding behavior around a stable simulation; it is not a recommendation to build this case on the RCT engine. <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2/develop/distribution/scripting/scripting.md">Official scripting documentation</a>.</p>
<h2>Prototype gates and failure tests</h2>
<p>These are proposed acceptance criteria, not measured results.</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Ordinary-play test:</strong> give six participants a three-minute production scenario without anomalies. At least four voluntarily try a second plan, and can explain its tradeoff. Failure means the management loop needs work before more lore.</li>
<li><strong>Ontology test:</strong> after the slice, at least five distinguish client, project, site, supplier, and entity without coaching. Any recurring conflation is a model or representation failure.</li>
<li><strong>Settlement test:</strong> present returned-payment, legitimate pooled-payment, and duplicate-Paid examples. At least five correctly separate observation from inference in all three. No UI state may assert a recipient without its required record.</li>
<li><strong>False-join test:</strong> include the source&#39;s two same-date $25,000 transfers (p. 45). The model must retain two objects; amount/date matching alone must never merge them.</li>
<li><strong>Control test:</strong> ask who can view, initiate, approve, and administer one transaction at two dates. At least five answer from the control view without reading an organization chart.</li>
<li><strong>Agency test:</strong> two different capacity plans must produce materially different delivery/cash consequences in fiction; neither may change documentary facts. If outcomes differ only in dialogue, the game is still a slideshow.</li>
<li><strong>Nonadditivity test:</strong> combine statement, bank, bill, and return views. The system must refuse a total across overlapping populations and explain the selected measure in plain language.</li>
<li><strong>Attention test:</strong> an eight-minute session allows at least three consequential operational actions before opening a long document. Essential facts remain recoverable without timed reading or mandatory pixel hunting.</li>
</ol>
<p>The next design choice is not which roof tiles to use. It is whether ordinary production planning remains enjoyable when the payment exception is removed. Test that and the evidence distinctions together; then choose the campaign&#39;s architecture.</p>
<h2>Research access and scope</h2>
<p>All linked public sources accessed 16 September 2026. External research used game/design/tax terminology only; no private case text was searched or uploaded. The primary case review was local. Public source scopes: Sawyer pages are creator statements; OpenRCT2 files are the current <code>develop</code> reconstruction and must be pinned to a revision before implementation; Indie Stone is a historical developer account; Church, MDA, Jenkins, and Bogost are author-authored design/theory works; GDC citations support their published abstracts only; mod pages support the creators&#39; descriptions and posted permissions; the IRS link is explicitly the 2024 edition. Drew1440 is secondary commentary. None of these sources independently validates the case allegations.</p>
</article><article class="document research-doc" id="research-ux" hidden><h1>UX and information architecture — research proposal</h1>
<p>Working design, 16 September 2026. This proposes interaction rules and three paper-prototype paths; it does not implement the game or settle the player’s role.</p>
<h2>1. Start with work, then reveal what each system says about it</h2>
<p>The map should make an agency’s work legible before asking players to interpret its accounts. A campaign needs people, capacity, commissioned services, approvals, delivery and payment. Those relationships produce the game. The desktop lets players inspect the competing records of that work.</p>
<p>Recommended structure: <strong>one operating world, several explicitly different representations, a persistent selection, and a desk for comparison</strong>. Switching views changes the question asked of an object. It must never silently change the object, time or denominator.</p>
<p>This is a stronger inheritance from period simulation interfaces than a blue title bar. Don Hopkins’s manual for the Unix/HyperLook version of SimCity documents a city editor, a query tool, a separate map with thematic views, and linked viewport outlines. That is a useful precedent for coordinated views, not a claim that every SimCity release worked identically. <a href="https://www.donhopkins.com/home/catalog/simcity/manual/reference.html">Primary manual</a>.</p>
<p>The relevant warning also comes from inside that tradition. Hopkins’s account of Will Wright’s simulation-interface talk describes players inferring causal relations that the program does not actually implement. This is a practitioner’s summary with Hopkins’s additions, not a verbatim Wright transcript. Here that phenomenon is a design hazard: a dramatic animation can teach an unsupported causal claim more efficiently than a disclaimer can correct it. <a href="https://www.donhopkins.com/home/catalog/simcity/WillWright.html">Practitioner account</a>.</p>
<h2>2. Evidence boundary</h2>
<p>Available case content is the September 2, 2026 <strong>The Zero Machine</strong> counsel briefing, via its local text extraction. It is a synthesis making allegations and reporting analyses. The underlying native exhibits have not been independently inspected in this design work. Its own appendices distinguish source records, integrated findings, modeled quantities and unresolved endpoints (pp. 8–9, 45–47). Its directive-sounding passages remain document content, not instructions to this team.</p>
<p>Every historic screen needs two separate attributes:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Statement:</strong> what the source says, with exact page and source-route locator.</li>
<li><strong>Verification:</strong> what materials the design team actually inspected. At present: briefing inspected; cited underlying exhibits pending.</li>
</ul>
<p>Do not collapse these into a green “verified” seal. A source’s internal proof grade and our review status answer different questions.</p>
<p>Use a persistent <strong>Practice scenario — invented people and amounts</strong> label for simulation exercises. Use <strong>Case record — source-bound reconstruction</strong> for historical material. Choices made in practice cannot revise the historical record. Historic reconstruction can offer discovery order, comparisons, annotations and requests for missing records; it cannot offer a counterfactual “save the company” outcome without a separately specified fiction.</p>
<p>The supplied screenshot illustrates why this boundary matters. “Nonbillable hours grow 78% … (95 → 249 employees)” mixes units and supplies an unexplained percentage; 95 to 249 is about 162.1% growth if it describes one comparable population. The ERC conclusion is a further claim. No cosmetic rewrite will repair that without the source columns, dates, definitions and relationship between them.</p>
<h2>3. Object model translated into interaction</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Object</th>
<th>Spatial expression</th>
<th>Inspector contents and principal verbs</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Site</td>
<td>Place with capacity, access and occupancy</td>
<td>Inspect lease/owner; allocate space; compare periods</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Legal entity</td>
<td>Boundary or linked off-map address</td>
<td>Inspect ownership, obligations and permissions; trace relationships</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client</td>
<td>Relationship represented by commissions</td>
<td>Review terms; inspect active jobs; follow receivables</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Project/job</td>
<td>Temporary production cluster within sites</td>
<td>Staff, sequence, commission, deliver; inspect associated records</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Employee</td>
<td>Member of a department with commitments</td>
<td>Allocate work; inspect workload and employment records</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Supplier/freelancer</td>
<td>Counterparty linked to commissioned work</td>
<td>Request service; inspect obligation, identity and payment lifecycle</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Obligation</td>
<td>Commitment attached to work and counterparties</td>
<td>Inspect terms; compare invoice, payment and settlement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment attempt</td>
<td>Dated event with its own identity</td>
<td>Trace funding, return, void, reissue and settlement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record/version</td>
<td>Inspectable document or system object</td>
<td>Open, compare, cite, pin; inspect origin and changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission grant</td>
<td>Time-bounded relationship to a system/action</td>
<td>Show who could view, approve, execute, edit, export or revoke</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>These categories may share artwork, but must not share identity. A freelancer can walk into the park like a visitor; economically they remain a supplier of work. A client representative is a visitor too, with a different relationship. “Guest” is therefore a presentation category, not the root of the financial model. A 1099 belongs to a tax-reporting relationship and period; it should not be a universal wearable accessory assigned from a character’s silhouette.</p>
<p>Similarly, an office is not a ride because it appears in an important episode. Place it when location, occupancy, lease burden or access changes play. An entity might own or occupy several sites, while a site may host several entities. Give shared-control relationships readable boundaries without implying geographic proximity establishes control.</p>
<p>Discussion with the systems designer added the permission object. A job title cannot substitute for access. The briefing’s responsibility-versus-authority passages make this distinction particularly important (pp. 10–16).</p>
<h2>4. The five views</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>View</th>
<th>Main question</th>
<th>Marks shown</th>
<th>Explicit limits</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Work</td>
<td>What is happening and what blocks delivery?</td>
<td>Project stages, dependencies, queues, capacity</td>
<td>Activity is not revenue or cash</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Cash</td>
<td>What transfer is observed, attempted or unresolved?</td>
<td>Bank events and payment lifecycles; a separate book-state strip</td>
<td>Paid is a platform label, not settlement proof</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Information</td>
<td>What record exists, where did it come from, what changed?</td>
<td>Documents, lineage, versions and missing links</td>
<td>A missing export is not evidence of deletion</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Control</td>
<td>Who could do which action, in which system, at that time?</td>
<td>Explicit permission edges and approval gates</td>
<td>Capability is not proof of an action or physical-user identity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Who does the work and bears the interruption?</td>
<td>Department load, commitments, service dependencies</td>
<td>Do not infer real people’s emotions, knowledge or motives</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Persistent header: selected object, viewed period, scenario type, active view. Time has two coordinates when necessary: <strong>event date</strong> and <strong>record version/as-of date</strong>. The bank event may be old while its platform representation changes later. A single date slider cannot faithfully express both.</p>
<p>The view switch retains selection, camera, zoom, period, pinned comparisons and navigation history. If an object has no representation in a view, keep its inspector open and say why. Do not drop it or quietly substitute its parent. A breadcrumb reads, for example, <code>Project C-07 / Obligation O-19 / Attempt P-04</code>.</p>
<p>Use solid connectors for supported relationships, dashed connectors for hypotheses, and visible broken ends for missing links. Explain every connector in words on selection. Do not animate speculative cash to an inferred beneficiary. Beyond bank-confirmed settlement, terminate at the last supported address.</p>
<p>Distinguish <strong>zero</strong>, <strong>not recorded</strong>, <strong>not supplied</strong>, <strong>not applicable</strong>, <strong>modeled</strong> and <strong>not yet inspected</strong>. In particular, the briefing’s 2024 input is N/A while its computation uses a zero for a limited model purpose (p. 9). Those must not become the same empty cell.</p>
<p>Totals require a population label, unit, period and basis. Selecting overlapping populations does not produce an automatic grand total. State “These selections overlap” and offer a deduplicated episode view. The briefing expressly prohibits adding its parallel statement, payment, bank and mutation surfaces as damages (pp. 9, 46).</p>
<p>Shneiderman’s 1996 visual-information taxonomy includes relation, history and extraction as well as overview, zoom, filtering and detail. Those less-quoted operations matter here: players need to preserve a trail and take a comparison away, not simply reveal another tooltip. <a href="https://www.cs.umd.edu/users/ben/papers/Shneiderman1996eyes.pdf">Original paper</a>.</p>
<h2>5. Three core paths</h2>
<h3>Path A — Make one campaign work</h3>
<p><strong>Purpose:</strong> establish the ordinary economy before introducing an anomaly. <strong>Setting:</strong> synthetic practice scenario, invented $100,000 client budget.</p>
<ol>
<li>Accept a small commission with three deliverables and a visible due date. Open its terms in place; no introductory accounting lecture.</li>
<li>Place a project cluster inside available studio space. Compare in-house production, which occupies staff and equipment, with an independent fabricator’s $12,000 component and longer lead time. Choose fabrication for this tutorial; preview capacity and contractual commitments first.</li>
<li>Run one planning interval. The world responds: the production queue clears or backs up, a handoff succeeds or waits, the department load changes. The player’s arrangement remains visible.</li>
<li>Inspect a delay. The work inspector names the dependency and offers <code>Reschedule</code>, <code>Reserve alternative capacity</code>, or <code>Inspect obligation</code>.</li>
<li>Deliver the first component. Compare completed work, invoiced work and received money on one short receipt. They need not move together.</li>
</ol>
<p>Pleasure comes from arranging a small working system and watching it function. Authored choices concern sequence, slack and resource commitments, not every footpath. The first lesson is that productive activity has its own identity; the accounts are representations of it.</p>
<h3>Path B — A payment returned; the interface still says Paid</h3>
<p><strong>Purpose:</strong> discriminate states, choose a useful next record, and manage the operational consequence. Continue the synthetic campaign.</p>
<ol>
<li>An unobtrusive project notice says the fabricated component is held. Its obligation shows platform status <code>Paid</code>, with settlement <code>Not yet checked</code>.</li>
<li>Pin the obligation and open the payment workbench. Compare a $12,000 debit, an exact $12,000 return and the surviving Paid label. Three facts; no verdict button.</li>
<li>Link the return to the existing attempt. The lifecycle now reads <code>Attempted → returned</code>; the obligation remains open for resolution. The game does not count a second loss or fabricate another payment.</li>
<li>Choose the next information request: processor history, recipient confirmation or an unrelated invoice export. Each explains what it can establish; responses can be pending, unavailable or confirmed. Missing settlement evidence alone does not prove nonpayment.</li>
<li>Choose an operational response: reserve alternative capacity for an option fee, defer the handoff, or revise the production sequence. Keep available cash, deadline and supplier commitment separately visible. Delays and tradeoffs belong to this practice model; they imply no historical opportunity to rescue the company.</li>
<li>Encounter a legitimate pooled funding example. A correctly supported batch must remain ordinary even though several records share one bank debit. Reward the accurate distinction, not the number of suspicious items found.</li>
</ol>
<p>After this mechanic is understood, offer a documentary comparison: the briefing’s Creative Peaks episode reports a $23,500 debit and return with PaidInFull retained; its Reddit example reports $50,000 of payment stories around one $25,000 debit (p. 34). Display these as the briefing’s claims with exhibit status pending. The cited corrections include ordinary pooled allocations and bank-supported pairs (pp. 45–46). Those controls should be playable checks, not legal small print.</p>
<h3>Path C — Same work, different record</h3>
<p><strong>Purpose:</strong> understand control and lineage without making bookkeeping a memorization test. <strong>Setting:</strong> source-bound historical reconstruction once exhibits are available; synthetic substitute until then.</p>
<ol>
<li>Select a project and compare two dated presentations. Their totals or identities differ; the selected economic work stays fixed.</li>
<li>Open Information view. Pin source and successor side by side, highlighting retained fields and missing fields separately.</li>
<li>Switch to Control with the same record selected. See title, responsibility and granted actions in separate rows. The useful question becomes “Who could export this version?” rather than “Who is the villain?”</li>
<li>Inspect an audit event. A named account is labeled an account; person attribution stays unresolved unless separately supported. Request authentication/session evidence when needed.</li>
<li>Assemble a short finding from linked observations: <code>These fields changed</code>; <code>this account is recorded</code>; <code>this question remains open</code>. The interface blocks no opinion, but keeps personal annotations visually separate from source statements.</li>
<li>Return to the same place and time on the map. A bookmark stores the comparison and can be reopened or exported with citations.</li>
</ol>
<p>This path gives research actions consequence: a better-supported explanation and a navigable reconstruction. It should not pretend that discovering one field grants the power to reverse a real migration or prevent a historical closure.</p>
<h2>6. Low-fidelity screens</h2>
<p>These are layout specifications, not final style. At desktop size, the scene and inspector remain co-visible. At narrower widths, the inspector becomes a full-height sheet with an explicit return target.</p>
<pre><code class="language-text">WORKSPACE · Practice scenario                   [Pause] [Next interval]
Project C-07     Week 3     [Work Cash Information Control People]
┌───────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
│                                   │ C-07 / Fabricated component  │
│ Studio + production clusters      │ Waiting on supplier release  │
│ Selected cluster outlined         │                              │
│ Department activity is visible    │ Due: Thursday                │
│                                   │ Obligation: $12,000           │
│ [mini-map]                        │ Platform: Paid               │
│                                   │ Settlement: Not yet checked  │
│                                   │ [Inspect obligation]         │
│                                   │ [Reserve capacity]           │
└───────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

</code></pre>
<pre><code class="language-text">PAYMENT WORKBENCH · O-19 · P-04            Practice / Week 3 / Paused
┌──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
│ Obligation           │ Bank events          │ Platform record     │
│ Fabrication $12,000   │ Debit   $12,000      │ Label: Paid         │

</code></pre>

┌──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
│ Earlier record               │ Later record                      │
│ Date / amount / project      │ Date / amount / [project missing]  │
│ [Exact source and locator]   │ [Exact source and locator]        │
└──────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘

Personal note: ___          [Save comparison] [Return to selection]
</code></pre>
<h2>7. BILL belongs in the workbench</h2>
<p>Bill payment is an instrument acting on obligations. An office or finance department is a place/capacity/control arrangement. Confusing them would imply that walking to a kiosk determines payment authority, or that buying another kiosk repairs missing settlement evidence.</p>
<p>A finance building can be a useful map entry point, with a visible queue if review capacity is part of the simulation. Every relevant obligation should still open the same workbench directly. Software administration, approval, execution and bank authority are distinct gates within it. Account 1072 must appear as a ledger clearing account, never a cash-storage tank or bank balance; the briefing explicitly makes that distinction (p. 1).</p>
<p>Beaudouin-Lafon’s 2000 instrumental-interaction model distinguishes domain objects from the tools mediating action on them. This gives a concrete test: an obligation is the object; comparison, tracing and payment scheduling are tools; a building represents a place or capability. <a href="https://www.lri.fr/~mbl/papers/CHI2000/">Author’s paper</a>.</p>
<h2>8. A desktop worth using</h2>
<p>Use a shallow desk with three arrangements: <code>World + inspector</code>, <code>Compare</code>, and <code>Reading</code>. Let players pin a small set of documents, save an arrangement and return without cleaning up a pile of windows. Kandogan and Shneiderman’s Elastic Windows research explored grouping and manipulating related windows; its 1995 scenario is a relevant period precedent for workspace organization. Our proposal is restrained tiling, not a reproduction. <a href="https://www.cs.umd.edu/projects/hcil/elastic-windows/scenario.html">Primary scenario</a>.</p>
<p>Documents arrive through a visible task, colleague, record request or archive location. Each acquisition changes what the player can inspect; it need not trigger a modal. Quotes such as the referenced “no yappin” messages are discoverable source artifacts only after the actual record is supplied. Do not manufacture the message, its sender, date, context or significance from the prompt.</p>
<p>Use a small contextual action menu with stable verbs—Inspect, Follow, Compare, Pin—also exposed in the inspector. Hopkins’s 1998 account of his Sims interface describes object-relative action menus and a face identifying the acting character. The transferable lesson is to keep action and actor clear. A radial menu is optional and must earn its space in testing. <a href="https://www.donhopkins.com/home/catalog/piemenus/NaturalSelection.html">Designer’s account</a>.</p>
<p>Density should unfold through dependency. Introduce a term when the player needs it, offer a one-line definition, and keep the fuller reference one step away. Never hide the qualification necessary to read a number correctly. Advanced controls may wait; “modeled” cannot.</p>
<h2>9. Tests before visual polish</h2>
<p>Build paper or clickable-screen tests of the three paths, using synthetic fixtures. Recruit six to eight participants spanning management-game familiarity and financial familiarity; use results diagnostically, not as a statistically representative verdict.</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>State discrimination:</strong> include paid-and-settled, paid-but-returned, legitimate pooled funding, duplicate records without duplicate cash, and unresolved endpoints. Can players explain each without inferring theft from a label? Any systematic error stops content production.</li>
<li><strong>View continuity:</strong> ask participants to follow one obligation across all five views, then return to the original project. Record lost selections, mistaken dates and mistaken object substitutions.</li>
<li><strong>Playable agency:</strong> first test three minutes with no anomaly. Allow two valid production plans with different costs. Ask players to identify consequences without reading a final explanation. If all meaningful action occurs in notices, the loop needs redesign.</li>
<li><strong>Source discipline:</strong> ask what was established by an exhibit, asserted by the briefing, inferred by the player, and invented for practice. Confusing these categories is a release blocker.</li>
<li><strong>Retention:</strong> the following day, ask for a sketch of work → obligation → payment attempt → settlement → record. The target is a usable distinction, not recall of names or totals.</li>
</ol>
<p>Provide keyboard operation and a nonspatial object list alongside the map. Reading and comparison pause simulation by default. Scale text independently of sprites; never force pixel fonts on long documents. Use labels/patterns as well as color, reduced motion, adjustable sound, visible focus and no hover-only evidence. The interface can look composed and particular without making accuracy depend on perfect vision, fast reading or a steady mouse.</p>
<p>The immediate design gate is small: can someone run one piece of work, discover one record conflict, and choose a sensible response while maintaining those distinctions? Until that works, a larger park will mostly give the confusion somewhere to walk.</p>
</article><article class="document research-doc" id="research-visual" hidden><h1>Visual craft research and direction tests</h1>
<p>Research date: 16 September 2026. Working proposals, not an approved art direction. Public research concerns game craft and asset production only; no case material was uploaded or used in public searches.</p>
<h2>Recommendation</h2>
<p>Test <strong>Campus 1998</strong> first: a small, carefully modelled agency world with the legibility of a late-1990s management game, inside a custom institutional desktop. Use fixed orthographic renders for the buildings and people, a separate crisp interface for reading, and reversible map views for following work, money, records and authority. The distinctive part should be what the picture lets the player understand.</p>
<p>Keep <strong>Production Floor</strong> as a serious alternative, especially if the game becomes a more explicit interactive infographic. Keep <strong>After Hours</strong> as the third comparison, not the inevitable dark mode. All three must render the same scene and support the same task before any can win. Different content would make the comparison meaningless.</p>
<p>The date in each direction names a visual grammar. It does not relocate the events to the 1990s. A contemporary document remains a contemporary document. A discovered ChatGPT exchange should preserve its real date and source identity, rather than becoming a fabricated message from an old computer.</p>
<h2>What the references actually support</h2>
<h3>RCT: modelled objects, controlled projection, very deliberate reduction</h3>
<p>Chris Sawyer&#39;s own gallery states that most RCT graphics began as 3D models and were pre-rendered at game scale. The enlarged models show geometry and detail that barely survive the final image. The useful lesson is to compose for the final size: roof silhouette, entrance, machinery and movement must read before decorative detail. It is inaccurate to describe this simply as hand-drawn “8-bit art.” <a href="https://www.chrissawyergames.com/feature3.htm">Chris Sawyer, graphics up close</a></p>
<p>OpenRCT2&#39;s Graphics Helper is a practical reference for locked cameras, lighting and repeatable output. Its published README specifies Blender 2.79 and a scanline renderer; it is not a ready-made add-on for current Blender. Treat its pipeline as reference material. A new project can use current tools, but must demonstrate its own visual consistency. <a href="https://github.com/OpenRCT2/Blender-RCT-Graphics">OpenRCT2 Graphics Helper</a></p>
<p>The OpenGraphics ride guide also makes the cost of optionality visible: angles, vehicles and riders multiply the required renders. Its own notice says the described process is outdated. The enduring production lesson is to separate recolour masks, occluding geometry and actors; do not adopt the obsolete instructions as a current build recipe. <a href="https://github.com/OpenRCT2/OpenGraphics/wiki/Building-a-ride">OpenGraphics ride guide</a></p>
<h3>Project Zomboid: ordinary things with useful state</h3>
<p>The Indie Stone&#39;s 2014 account describes a pipeline able to produce both sprite frames and mesh/animation data. Its 2023 engine discussion describes 2D environmental tiles, depth information and cached chunks, with experimental qualifications. Those are dated accounts of development, not a claim that every proposed feature shipped. The lesson for this project is that familiar sprite appearance and richer spatial information can coexist; the world need not become a freely rotating 3D scene to support useful occlusion and lighting. <a href="https://projectzomboid.com/blog/news/2014/01/body-movin/">Sprite of the Living Dead</a>, <a href="https://projectzomboid.com/blog/news/2023/02/play-your-cardz-right/">Play Your Cardz Right</a></p>
<p>The two supplied mods are different kinds of reference:</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Supplied reference</th>
<th>Verified identity and terms</th>
<th>Useful design lesson</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td><a href="https://www.nexusmods.com/projectzomboid/mods/37">Nexus mod 37</a></td>
<td><strong>Item Icons – Modders Resource</strong>, Incariuz. Page describes 341 icons and four templates. Permission is limited to Project Zomboid mods; some images derive from game assets.</td>
<td>A coherent inventory can distinguish many mundane objects without making each one a spectacle. Study silhouette, container shape and category consistency. Do not import these icons into this game.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://steamcommunity.com/workshop/filedetails/?id=2337452747">Steam item 2337452747</a></td>
<td><strong>Daddy Dirkie dirks tiles</strong>, mod ID <strong>Diederiks Tile Palooza</strong>. A tile dependency library, not a standalone scenario. Description permits credited use in maps and prohibits redistribution/editing without written permission. A later author comment discusses community continuation, but does not establish permission for this separate game.</td>
<td>A shared building vocabulary supports many authored places. Walls, thresholds, furniture and signs can be recombined while behaviour comes from data. Study it; do not assume Workshop availability grants reuse rights.</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>The Steam extraction also exposed incompatible/removed banners alongside the listing. That is insufficient to determine present functionality. No mod was installed or tested.</p>
<h3>Poolside.fm: a designed operating system, not a screenshot of one</h3>
<p>In the designers&#39; account, Poolside.fm&#39;s target was an invented period operating system, with hand-pixelled icons and carefully handled transitions. That is the right permission to take: keep the recognisable rules of windows, focus, menus, files and workspaces, while designing the shapes and identity specifically for this agency world. Its cocktails and leisure branding have no useful job here. <a href="https://www.commarts.com/webpicks/poolside-fm">Design-team account in Communication Arts</a></p>
<h3>Supplied local references</h3>
<p>The local JPG is the cover of Mike Wilks&#39;s <strong>The Weather Works</strong>. Visually inspected. Major structural bays hold many small mechanisms; pipes and rails make motion inferable; quieter framing keeps the machinery from becoming one undifferentiated tangle. Borrow the hierarchy and the visible handoff between machines, not the book&#39;s architecture or illustrations.</p>
<p>The <strong>Garden</strong> HTML was inspected in source and in a browser. Its source maps record type to plant form, status to bloom/coverage, and <code>produces[]</code> to roots. In the rendered view, the same outline remains stable while colour plates and readouts change. That is an unusually relevant precedent: form is generated from the taxonomy. Its tiny type and document-sized central figure should not transfer to the game. The playable world needs more space.</p>
<ul>
<li>Garden: <a href="/Users/juliacompton/Downloads/garden-standalone_1.html">local reference</a>.</li>
<li>Wilks cover: <a href="/Users/juliacompton/Library/Mobile%20Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/ICLOUD-bridge/Codex/Games/Mad%20Money%20Tycoon/Refs/095c657c898b32b5a007b263536722ac.jpg">local reference</a>.</li>
<li>The AVIF map was subsequently decoded and inspected by the integrating review: a forested landscape, sparse built areas and a visible dimetric grid. It is useful for comparing geographic scale and map orientation. Its game identity and relevance to any case location have not been established.</li>
</ul>
<h2>Three direction specifications</h2>
<p>These are design proposals. Palettes are starting values for proofing, not a claim of tested contrast. Interface type remains scalable; world pixels do not dictate text size. IBM Plex is a practical family candidate with Sans, Serif and Mono sources in its official repository; ship the appropriate font licence with whichever files are eventually used. <a href="https://github.com/IBM/plex">IBM Plex</a></p>
<h3>A. Campus 1998 — recommended first test</h3>
<p><strong>Character.</strong> A business park designed by someone who believed its stationery. Pleasant at first glance, specific under inspection. Moss lawns, concrete walkways, low offices, furniture just visible through cutaway walls. The dry humour comes from a perfectly ordinary organisation developing increasingly extraordinary plumbing.</p>
<p><strong>Era grammar.</strong> Late-1990s construction simulation plus a custom corporate workstation. Small, solid, shaded world objects; restrained bevels on genuinely pressable controls; compact tool strips; independent inspectors. Avoid a literal Windows title bar, fake system error icons and large modal interruptions.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Element</th>
<th>Concrete specification</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Palette</td>
<td>Paper <code>#E8E3D4</code>; ink <code>#25332F</code>; lawn <code>#78936B</code>; roof <code>#526657</code>; concrete <code>#C7C8B8</code>; brass <code>#BBA15C</code>; brick <code>#B95740</code>. Use brass as identity/selection trim, not a universal warning colour.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Type</td>
<td>IBM Plex Sans for interface and body; Plex Mono for dates, amounts and short identifiers. 15–16 px reading text at standard desktop scale, tabular numerals. A compact custom pixel face may label signs inside the world only.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>World materials</td>
<td>Four to six controlled shade steps per material, quiet roof grain, opaque floor plates, small contact shadows. Brick expressed by a few grouped marks; no noisy photo texture.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Projection</td>
<td>Proposed 2:1 dimetric ground grid. One fixed view first; four discrete views only if occlusion testing proves necessary. Maintain a generous top surface for rooms and queues.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Window furniture</td>
<td>Thin dark keyline, pale inset content, 3 px hard shadow, short hatched focus grip. Active app identified by a side tab and title weight; no bright blue horizontal bar. Close/minimise symbols remain conventional.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Icons</td>
<td>20–24 px functional silhouettes, two or three tones, consistent upper-left light. A folder is a folder; a payment is not a cash-bag mascot. Signs and department symbols use the same drawing discipline.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Initially 24–32 source pixels tall, tested against 64×32 source-pixel ground tiles. Department encoded by a small badge/garment region plus inspector label; employment relationship never inferred from costume.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Motion</td>
<td>Eight-direction walk source, provisional six-frame cycles. Smooth movement between anchored positions; short held poses at meaningful handoffs. Production can look busy without every sprite vibrating.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Signature move</td>
<td>A roof lifts to a thin wire outline when its building is selected; the interior and its inbound/outbound queues remain in place. The same roof returns when the inspector closes.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Main risk</td>
<td>“Nice little campus” can trivialise human consequences. Fatigue, staffing pressure and unpaid work need clear inspectable states rather than slapstick distress animations.</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p><strong>Style-tile contents.</strong> One office with cutaway desks, one outside provider arriving, one waiting work packet, the selected building inspector, a linked document page, and one view switch. Show the same amount/date in the map label and document. Every sample number must be labelled synthetic until sourced data is integrated.</p>
<h3>B. Production Floor — the agency as a printed operating diagram</h3>
<p><strong>Character.</strong> Paste-up boards, job jackets, acetate overlays, press sheets and immaculate registration marks. The agency&#39;s own production language becomes the means of reading it. Warmer and more editorial than a terminal; more explicitly diagrammatic than a park.</p>
<p><strong>Era grammar.</strong> A consciously mixed 1992–1997 prepress workstation: flat work surfaces, palettes, rulers, pinned job windows and print-preview logic. This is an art-direction choice, not a reconstruction of one application.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Element</th>
<th>Concrete specification</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Palette</td>
<td>Stock <code>#EEEBDD</code>; key <code>#282621</code>; cobalt <code>#3F56A6</code>; coral <code>#CF6F66</code>; citron <code>#D8D270</code>; grey-violet <code>#AAA4B6</code>. Plates stay low-saturation enough for labels to read.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Type</td>
<td>Plex Sans for all controls; Plex Serif for explanatory case-reading panes; Plex Mono for docket metadata. Serif stays in documents, never on 12 px controls.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>World materials</td>
<td>Modelled forms reduced to three spot-colour families plus stable dark outlines. Very slight grain in large fills; broken hatch only on turned-away faces. No moving paper grain.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Projection</td>
<td>The same 2:1 grid as A, simplified roof/floor shapes, broader walkways. Objects can retain silhouette at a much lower detail level.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Window furniture</td>
<td>File tabs, ruled separators, punched index strip, 1 px keyline; no ornamental torn-paper edges. Pinned document slips stack at the side of a substantial viewport.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Icons</td>
<td>Filled cut-paper silhouettes inside a 24 px cell. Fine single-line registration marks are decorative only; hit targets remain conventional sized.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Motion</td>
<td>A plate arrives in two measured steps when a layer view changes. Keyline and text remain fixed. Job packets advance only when the underlying work state changes.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Signature move</td>
<td>Control, records and transfers become separate acetate-like overlays over an unchanged map. Showing a second layer adds a named legend; it does not mix colours into an unexplained brown soup.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Main risk</td>
<td>The world could feel like a beautifully animated report. Keep people, queues, agency over routing and consequential spatial choices visible; test whether the player still wants to arrange and revisit it.</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>The Garden informs the mechanism: registered structure and selectively separated colour. Do not copy its branding, historical prompts or editorial status rules. In particular, a shifted print plate must never imply a falsified document. Texture is not evidence.</p>
<h3>C. After Hours — a compact world inside a precise workstation</h3>
<p><strong>Character.</strong> Blue-green office darkness, pools of task light, warm screens, busy rooms still occupied. The most atmospheric candidate. It should feel like staying late, not like the interface already knows who is guilty.</p>
<p><strong>Era grammar.</strong> A late-1990s professional workstation with tiled inspectors and instrument panels, rather than a domestic OS imitation. Use clear mode indication and direct manipulation; not hacker glyphs or surveillance theatre.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Element</th>
<th>Concrete specification</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Palette</td>
<td>Deep green <code>#172D2A</code>; surface <code>#29443E</code>; fog <code>#9CB5A2</code>; warm document <code>#E9E2C8</code>; task light <code>#D5BC78</code>; soft rose <code>#C58378</code>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Type</td>
<td>Plex Sans for controls and narrative; Plex Mono sparingly for identifiers and time. A bright, warm document pane carries long reading. Do not typeset paragraphs in green monospace.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>World materials</td>
<td>The same simple forms as A, with independent light/emissive masks. Light falls in small stepped pools. Reflections limited to one or two pixels; no full-scene bloom haze.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Projection</td>
<td>Locked orthographic scene; darkened nonselected areas retain silhouettes and labels. Doors, queues and selected routes stay visible.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Window furniture</td>
<td>Flat green-grey panels, warm focus line, small square toggles, narrow labelled tracks. Border states replace glowing everything.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Icons</td>
<td>Pale single-tone silhouettes with a second tone on active controls. Any bright status uses a shape and label as well as colour.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Motion</td>
<td>Mostly still. A task lamp activates when work starts; a printer emits a sheet when a record is produced. A trace can be replayed at deliberate speed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Signature move</td>
<td>The player traces one transaction/work packet; local pools of light reveal its recorded handoffs without changing the map or inventing people who were present.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Main risk</td>
<td>Thriller lighting can imply malice before the evidence earns it. Also easiest to make inaccessible. Reject if a neutral interaction reads as sinister, or a route cannot be read with lighting effects disabled.</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<h2>A visual language that survives all three directions</h2>
<p>The map must have a declared syntax before artists populate it.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Thing being represented</th>
<th>Proposed visual rule</th>
<th>What the rule must not imply</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Place</td>
<td>Floor plate/building silhouette; address on selection</td>
<td>That the building is a legal entity, client or account</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Entity</td>
<td>Boundary/crest/registered-name label around its controlled nodes</td>
<td>That common branding establishes common legal ownership</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Work</td>
<td>A job packet with stage and capacity demand</td>
<td>That billed revenue equals completed work</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Person/team</td>
<td>Person markers grouped by department; staffing state in inspector</td>
<td>That every walking figure is a guest, contractor or employee</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record</td>
<td>Document object with source, date and version</td>
<td>That a later account is contemporaneous evidence</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Money</td>
<td>Directed route/transfer trace with amount, date and parties</td>
<td>That physical distance is payment delay, or that any moving dot is money</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Authority</td>
<td>Boundary plus labelled approval/signing relationship</td>
<td>That awareness, access and legal control are interchangeable</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Unknown or disputed</td>
<td>Explicit status label and an explainable gap in the relationship</td>
<td>That obscurity, red paint or visual corruption proves misconduct</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Views should change emphasis, not geography. A selected entity, zoom, time interval and open document persist when switching between work, money, records and control. Two views may be compared; four simultaneous bright flow fields will be a screensaver.</p>
<p>Use redundant encodings: money has arrowed double lines and amount labels; work has packets/stage marks; records have document corners and a dotted trail; control has named boundaries and approval links. Palette values can change between themes, but category identity and label wording cannot. A dashed relationship must have one meaning within a view; it cannot mean both “unverified” and “information.”</p>
<p>The supplied screenshot demonstrates a failure to prevent: it places an hours percentage next to an employee-count change without explaining their relationship. Every displayed quantity needs a measure, unit, period, population and source. A neat label can still make a false claim. A player should be able to open the denominator, not just dismiss the announcement.</p>
<h2>Pipeline comparison</h2>
<p>All figures below are provisional production specifications, not measurements from a built game.</p>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Route</th>
<th>Strength</th>
<th>Actual cost</th>
<th>When to use</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Draw every sprite by hand</td>
<td>Maximum control over silhouette and edge placement; very compact result</td>
<td>Every angle/state/body variation becomes drawing work; inconsistent perspective is expensive to repair</td>
<td>Bespoke icons, tiny people, exceptional props, final pixel cleanup</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Model → orthographic sprite sheets</td>
<td>Repeatable projection, interchangeable office kits, cheap recolours, familiar management-game image</td>
<td>Render setup, palette conversion, atlases and sort/occlusion rules need discipline; many directions multiply storage</td>
<td>Preferred base route for buildings, furnishings and repeatable actors</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Live orthographic 3D</td>
<td>Camera flexibility, cutaways, dynamic light and simple physical depth</td>
<td>More camera/art/performance QA, increased temptation to overmodel, pixel shimmer unless controlled</td>
<td>Choose only if user-controlled rotation or height is central to the actual game</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Sprites plus optional depth/normal/mask data</td>
<td>Keeps authored image while allowing selected lighting, highlights and occlusion</td>
<td>More asset exports, shader complexity, consistency checks; not a free upgrade</td>
<td>Small technical spike after the basic sprite scene proves useful</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<h3>Recommended production experiment</h3>
<ol>
<li>Make one small office kit: floor, two wall directions, doorway, desk, screen, chair, printer and a roof. Add two differently shaped buildings and a few human silhouettes. This is enough to test scale and occlusion.</li>
<li>Lock a projection contract. A 2:1 diamond is <strong>dimetric</strong>, with 26.565° ground diagonals; true equal-axis isometric has a different appearance. Do not mix packs with incompatible camera projections and hope the eye forgives it.</li>
<li>Start with 64×32 source-pixel floor tiles and 2× integer display, then test a denser 96×48 variant. Keep text in the full-resolution UI. These are competing prototypes, not an instruction to render the whole browser at low resolution.</li>
<li>Render base colour, alpha, contact shadow, recolour mask and object/part IDs separately. Store tile anchor, footprint, bounds and occluding parts in a manifest. Roof removal and selection then use the same model data.</li>
<li>Reduce using a controlled project palette. Inspect at actual on-screen size. Clean silhouettes and repeating windows by hand. A beautiful 2,000 px model does not rescue a mushy 60 px building.</li>
<li>Test movement behind a desk, past a doorway and between two buildings. Split large sprites into sensible front/back parts where required. Only add per-pixel depth if ordinary sorting cannot support the intended interactions.</li>
<li>Compare a completely unfiltered render, restrained ordered dither and a restricted light mask. Select by readability and mood; “more effects” is not a criterion.</li>
<li>Package geometry and visual definitions independently from case events. A lease, a tenant and a legal entity must be able to change without repainting a building.</li>
</ol>
<p>Modern Blender is a plausible authoring tool. Orthographic projection removes perspective convergence and is documented by Blender; exact render-version settings still need a local proof. Do not promise the historical RCT helper will run inside a current installation. <a href="https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/4.2/editors/3dview/navigate/projections.html">Blender projection documentation</a></p>
<h3>Dither and light: use them where they do a job</h3>
<p>The library&#39;s W2 implementation genuinely connects shading treatment to scene depth; it adds the ordered threshold before quantization. Its source comments distinguish procedural depth in the example from a real depth texture in the original. Borrow that honesty along with the technique. In an orthographic office map, generic screen height is not a legitimate substitute for visibility, importance or certainty.</p>
<p>For this game, trial a small Bayer pattern only in broad material shading and lighting falloff. Anchor it to the source-pixel grid so camera motion does not turn surfaces into crawling noise. Mask out text, monetary labels, selection outlines, people and documents. Disable it entirely at distant zoom. If the source has already been palette-quantized, avoid applying a second full-screen quantizer that destroys category colours.</p>
<p>Selection may reduce environmental detail around the chosen object, but an alleged entity must not become “corrupted” merely because it is alleged. Visibility and evidence confidence are different variables. Provide a no-texture option and reduced motion; these must preserve the same information.</p>
<h3>Art budgets need multiplication, not optimism</h3>
<p>A provisional human set with eight directions, six walk frames and three genuinely different silhouettes already requires 144 frames before idle/work variants. Recolour masks can avoid duplicating every shirt. They cannot make identical silhouettes distinguishable. Department is a functional tag, not a separate fully animated body type.</p>
<p>A 2048×2048 RGBA atlas is 16 MiB uncompressed before mipmaps or extra masks. A second full-size normal/depth texture can erase the apparent savings of “just sprites.” Measure atlas occupancy, rendering time and frame time on the intended browser/hardware before committing to those features.</p>
<h2>Reusable assets: useful starting material, not a visual direction</h2>
<div class="table-scroll"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Candidate</th>

<th>Fit and limits</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Kenney City Kit (Commercial)</td>
<td><a href="https://kenney.nl/assets/city-kit-commercial">Official pack</a>, 50 files, CC0 at research date</td>
<td>Suitable for projection/scale tests and background city massing. “Commercial” names the building category; the page separately states the licence. Too generic to stand in for every important address unchanged.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Kenney Furniture Kit</td>
<td><a href="https://kenney.nl/assets/furniture-kit">Official pack</a>, 140 files, CC0</td>
<td>Desk/chair/room blocking candidates. Rescale, recolour and re-render under one rig; inspect silhouettes at target resolution.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Kenney Isometric Tiles City</td>
<td><a href="https://kenney.nl/assets/isometric-tiles-city">Official pack</a>, 128 files, CC0</td>
<td>Fast 2D projection experiment. Do not mix its existing raster projection directly with a differently rendered 3D office kit.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Synty POLYGON Office</td>
<td><a href="https://syntystore.com/products/polygon-office-pack">Official pack</a>, office props, modular pieces, characters, FBX/OBJ source formats</td>
<td>Broad functional coverage. Stock proportions and faceted surfaces are conspicuous, so use as a kit to art-direct, not a finished identity. No animations included. Paid licence; none purchased.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Original RCT/PZ artwork and the supplied mods</td>
<td>Creator/game/mod sources above</td>
<td>Craft references. No assumption that installing a game/mod permits extraction into this separate project.</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Synty&#39;s FAQ permits commercial game use subject to the relevant EULA and prohibits reselling the assets as rendered image files. A distributable sprite/mod kit needs a different rights review from a finished game. Use CC0 or commissioned assets for public-facing example packs until the intended distribution is checked. This is why the licence field belongs in the asset manifest. <a href="https://syntystore.com/community/faq">Synty FAQ</a></p>
<p>No packs were downloaded or benchmarked in this research pass. Product listings establish availability and stated terms, not suitability or production quality.</p>
<h2>Mod support without turning the case into mush</h2>
<p>Use the mod references as architecture clues. Visual kits, maps and authored stories are different extension types.</p>
<ul>
<li>A <strong>visual kit</strong> changes furniture/tiles/icons and declares its projection, palette and licence. It cannot silently change a party&#39;s legal identity or a transaction amount.</li>
<li>A <strong>scenario</strong> supplies its own parties, event data, claims and source records under a distinct scenario ID. The supplied case remains a named source dataset, not a loose pile of replaceable strings.</li>
<li>A <strong>rule variation</strong> can explore a counterfactual with an explicit label and a fresh save identity. It cannot pass itself off as the recorded sequence.</li>
<li>A <strong>view extension</strong> reads typed state and adds an explanation, overlay or export. The original records remain reachable.</li>
</ul>
<p>For the first prototype, this means boring but useful structure: namespaced stable IDs, schema versions, separate asset manifests, explicit dependencies and a visible modified-scenario label. It does not mean building a Workshop, scripting sandbox or multiplayer economy now.</p>

<h2>Next review: three equal tests</h2>
<p>Each direction gets one 1440×900 still and a 15–20 second motion sample of the <strong>same synthetic scene</strong>: an office, two departments, one external provider, one job packet, one transfer and a linked source document. Hold layout, labels and figures constant. Then ask the reviewer to locate the provider, describe the work bottleneck, identify who may approve the transfer and open the record supporting it.</p>
<p>Reject a direction if people mistake places for entities, visual texture for uncertainty, or animation for proof that an event happened. Reject a pipeline if changing a named party or address requires repainting the whole scene. Reject a UI if reading the document loses the map, time position or selection.</p>
<p>The art decision should follow those tests. A beige window can be very handsome and still be wrong.</p>
<h2>Accompanying studies and verification</h2>
<p><a href="/Users/juliacompton/Library/Mobile%20Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/ICLOUD-bridge/Codex/Games/Mad%20Money%20Tycoon/Design/research/visual-assets/tiles.html">Open the three-direction contact sheet</a>. It holds the same synthetic scene and inspector layout across all three proposals. These are original SVG geometry studies with system-font substitutes, not Blender renders, finished sprites, functional UI or motion tests. Their purpose is to compare palette, figure/ground and document treatment before investing in production art.</p>
<p>All three were rendered and visually inspected in the browser at a 1502 px viewport. The contact sheet has three sections and no horizontal overflow there. A label collision and a background-tree occlusion error were corrected during inspection. The three <code>*-study.png</code> files in <code>visual-assets/</code> preserve the checked desktop views. Interaction, font deployment, accessibility contrast, alternate screen sizes and a real sprite export remain untested.</p>
